Chapter 17: That we look upon


Chapter Notes

😭 I know I say it every week but UGHHHH! Thank you all so much!!! Truly, your comments and support mean everything when writing such an emotionally challenging fic! (I’m working my way through replies!)

I have fallen in love with these characters so much and I’m so honoured to have you all along for their journey. As we know, it’s not easy but bit by bit they will get there!

The vote was an overwhelming YES for NottPott, that’s on the way one day this week and contains info on H and D that is definitely insight but can be skipped if it’s not your thing!

A little note: The giraffe toy Draco gives to Aurora this chapter is a little callback to my own daughter’s favourite chew toy (Sophie La Girafe) and a product you can still buy! I’m loving dropping in little self inserts here and there, reminding myself of those mental but beautiful baby days!

As always, thank you so much for reading!

Nicky xx



See the end of the chapter for more notes



The silence in the car was deafening.

After making her shocking declaration, Narcissa Malfoy had smiled softly as she drifted off to sleep, peaceful now that she’d remembered the name of the girl her son had been infatuated with at school.

Infatuated.

The word repeated in Hermione’s head on a refrain as Malfoy drove them away from The Grove, joining the constant echoes of Draco’s own words, already waiting in the recesses of her psyche where she’d tried to bury them.

‘You’re everything I thought you were.’

‘You aren’t less.’

‘I can think of a thousand other words that define who you are, but none of them could ever even begin to do you justice.’

She risked a sideways glance at Malfoy, who was staring ahead at the road, unblinking, his expression reminiscent of a man trying desperately not to vomit.

‘Thank you,’ she began tentatively, knowing that she’d need to be the one to break the awkward silence between them. ‘Thank you for letting me into this part of your life. I know that it must have been difficult.’

Feeling somewhat reckless, she reached over to put what she hoped was a comforting hand on his knee. Malfoy flinched at the contact, but then seemed to forcibly relax, taking his own hand off the wheel to reach down and cover hers. He didn’t say anything, and Hermione wondered how much he regretted taking her to meet Narcissa.

‘I’m so sorry about your mum.’ She continued. ‘I’m sorry you have so much to deal with.’

He swallowed hard, turning to glance at her before quickly flicking his eyes back to the road. ‘It’s alright.’ He shrugged, although Hermione was positive, after the way he’d arrived at her door earlier that night, that it definitely was not alright. ‘She’s been this way for such a long time now that I can barely even remember what she was like before.’

‘Does she have GMD?’ Hermione asked gently, having picked up on some of the symptoms Narcissa was displaying. Geriatric Magical Declination was a form of dementia often seen in the magical world, although usually in people far, far older than Narcissa Malfoy.

Malfoy grimaced. ‘Of a sort.’ He frowned for a moment before pressing on. ‘After I got married and my father died, my mother was alone a lot. She… she wasn't doing well, and she attempted to Obliviate herself. She wanted to get rid of certain… memories. But…it went wrong. She didn’t complete the spell. It left her with some cognitive damage, which can make her magic hard to control. Sometimes she’s lucid, but other times, she forgets where she is, who she is. She misremembers things.’

He’d said the last words meaningfully, and despite herself, Hermione’s heart sank. Malfoy was telling her, very clearly, that Narcissa often said things that weren’t true. She didn’t know why the confirmation hurt so much, why she’d even believed for a second that Malfoy might have had feelings for her at school. Of course he hadn’t. He’d just hated her for beating his grades. That made a lot more sense, of course.

‘Initially, there was hope for recovery.’ Malfoy continued, unaware of Hermione’s inner turmoil. ‘I had some of the most esteemed healers in the magical world looking for a way to reverse the Obliviation. But it’s been nearly ten years now, and she’s no better. Sometimes she gets scared and forgets where she is. When that happens, her magic goes crazy. She can be dangerous. When she really loses it, the only thing that can calm her is me. I-I use my Legillimency to get inside her head and talk her down from the inside…I’m gentle.’ He clarified, as though Hermione might doubt for a second that he would be. ‘I don’t hurt her. I just remind her where she is, that she’s safe. It calms her enough so that the Healers don’t have to stun her. I don’t like it when they do that.’

Hermione listened silently, her heart aching once again for Malfoy.

‘It’s harder now that I have Aurora.’ He said, turning off the motorway and heading towards the city. ‘I can’t just Apparate straight there when they call me. Mum was…’ his voice cracked slightly. ‘She was in a bad way when I arrived earlier. It’s- it’s really hard to see her so distressed. I try to tell myself that most of the time, she seems happy enough at The Grove. Happier than she was at the manor anyway, and much safer.’

‘You’re doing the right thing.’ Hermione told him with absolute conviction. ‘Sometimes we have to make difficult choices to keep the people we love safe. Even if it hurts us to do it.’

Malfoy looked sideways at her, clearly picking up on her tone. He waited, and without planning to Hermione suddenly found herself saying something she’d only ever admitted to her closest friends.

‘I… I actually Obliviated my own parents.’ She said shakily, hating the way the words sounded coming out of her mouth. ‘Thirteen years ago… I didn’t want them to become collateral in a war they didn’t understand, and I didn't want them to become a target as a way to get to me. Th-they live in Australia now. They don’t remember me, don’t remember they had a daughter at all.’

Malfoy had gone still, the hand that covered hers squeezing tighter.

‘Granger,’ he breathed. ‘Fuck. I'm sorry…I had no idea.’

Hermione felt tears spring into her eyes, her mouth suddenly dry. She hadn’t spoken about her parents in a very long time, not even with Harry - It was too painful.

‘Have you seen them since?’ Malfoy asked gently, the sensation of his thumb rubbing slowly over the back of her hand more comforting than Hermione thought he knew.

‘Once.’ She nodded. ‘Harry and I took a trip about five years ago. I’d finally had to accept that there was no safe way to reverse an Obliviation, but I thought, maybe they’d see me and they’d somehow know. I thought I could stage an accidental meeting, find a way to insert myself into their lives so that eventually, I could tell them who I really was.’

She blinked at the blurry lights passing by the windscreen. ‘But when I saw them, they looked so healthy and so happy that I just… I had to let them live. I didn’t l want to do anything that might inadvertently hurt them or cause them any distress. So I didn’t talk to them, I didn’t let them see me. I just came home and had to accept the fact that I no longer have any family…at all.’

Hermione didn’t even realise Malfoy was pulling the car over until he was reaching across the seat for her, gathering her towards him. She melted immediately into the embrace, too sad not to let herself give into his warmth, his clean scent, the almost unbearable rightness of being cocooned within his arms, so strong but so gentle. ‘I’m sorry,’ he murmured quietly, his fingers smoothing over her hair, his face pressed into the top of her head.‘I’m so sorry, Granger. You’re so fucking brave. You did the right thing too.’

Hermione closed her eyes against his chest and let Malfoy comfort her, dimly aware of the complete reversal of their roles from earlier that night. She had never intended to divulge something so deeply personal, but Draco had been so vulnerable with her over the past few hours, he felt like the only person she wanted to tell, the only person she wanted full stop.

When she finally managed to pull away, Hermione looked out of the window to find that they were parked up in a side street just a few hundred metres from her flat. Malfoy hadn’t waited to complete the journey. He’d known she needed his comfort there and then. The realisation made her heart hurt, aching with all the ways she just wanted more and more from him, so much that wasn’t hers to take.

He waited patiently for her to calm down before restarting the car and less than a minute later they were pulling up smoothly into the parking space outside the flat.

‘You’re sure you’re okay?’ He asked, dragging up the handbrake and turning to look at her.

Hermione nodded. ‘I’m sorry. I really didn’t mean to make that about me. You were trying to tell me about your mum.’

‘Don’t be ridiculous.’ He frowned. ‘There was nothing more to tell, and anyway, I can't always be the saddest bastard. Also…’ he peered at her from under a furrowed brow. ‘It sort of felt like you’ve been holding on to that for a long time.’

Hermione sighed. ‘Too long. I-I find it very difficult to talk about. Most of the time, I know I did the right thing, but it doesn't stop me from missing them terribly. It’s a strange feeling, mourning someone who’s still alive, you know?’

He didn’t respond, and Hermione realised that he did know. He probably understood better than anyone.

With the engine off, Aurora began to stir, squirming in her car seat for a few seconds before letting out a small, unhappy cry.

‘Shit.’ Malfoy glanced up at the glowing light on the dash. ‘She’s overdue for a feed. Probably a nappy change too.’

‘Bring her up to the flat,’ Hermione said quickly. ‘You can change and feed her there.’

Malfoy looked doubtful. ‘It’s only another few minutes to the Penthouse from here,’ he said, wincing as Aurora let out another plaintive cry.

‘What about Harry and Theo? They’ll be at the Penthouse.’

Malfoy opened his mouth and closed it again. ‘It’s three am,’ he said hesitantly. ‘I’m sure they’ll be… finished by now, won’t they?’

‘Can you guarantee that?’ Hermione raised a brow. ‘It’s their first date. What if you walk in right in the middle of the action? They left here talking about the jacuzzi. What if you accidentally get an eyeful of Theo’s bare bum… or Harry’s?’

Malfoy almost choked, visibly recoiling in horror as Hermione giggled.

‘Gods.’ He muttered. ‘You’re right. I can’t go back to the Penthouse.’

Hermione shrugged, unable to hide her pleased smile. ‘Well, you’ll have to come in then, won’t you?’



* * *



In her usual fashion, by the time they got up to the flat, Aurora had ramped up rapidly from 0-100 and was now sobbing loudly from her car seat, her face pink and screwed up. ‘It’s alright, my girl,’ Malfoy muttered, casting a silencing charm on the hallway as Hermione fumbled for her wand to unlock the door. ‘Daddy will feed you in just one second.’

Hermione eventually got the door open and they dived into the flat, Malfoy immediately dropping to his knees to unclick Aurora from the car seat. He pulled the screaming baby out and held her to his chest, clearly flustered by the volume of her cries.

‘Can I help?’ Hermione offered.

‘Nope.’ He didn’t look at her, beginning to root around in the baby bag while holding Aurora’s writhing body with his other arm. ‘I’m good.’

The brusqueness of his tone stung a little, but Hermione knew him well enough by now to understand this was how Malfoy got when Aurora was unhappy. He hated it.

‘Bollocks.’ He hissed. ‘I think I left her bottle in the car. I’ll need to go and fetch it.’

‘Don’t panic.’ Hermione reached for Aurora. ‘I’m here. I can wait with her for a moment.’

He dithered for a beat before nodding. ‘Okay. If you don’t mind.’

He handed Aurora to her carefully, pulling an apologetic face as she cried even louder, puce with indignation at the fact someone other than her father was holding her.

‘My goodness, Aurora,’ Hermione crooned. ‘You are fierce, aren’t you. Don’t worry, Daddy won’t be long.’ She rocked the baby gently, moving her up onto her shoulder and rubbing her back in slow soothing strokes.

Malfoy leaned in to plant a quick kiss on Aurora’s hot, red cheek. ‘I’ll be quick.’ He promised and then Disapparated with an alarmingly loud crack and a small twist of black smoke.

Hermione blinked at the empty space he’d occupied, realising that she’d never seen Malfoy Apparate before. It was… aggressive. Within seconds he was back, appearing in the same space with a full bottle of milk and a lilac muslin cloth.

He removed the stasis charm from the bottle and reached for Aurora, gently transferring her from Hermione’s arms into his own as he guided the teat to her mouth. Aurora latched on instantly, taking the milk down in huge, greedy gulps, her silver eyes still filled with tears. Hermione watched as Aurora reached up to clutch at the bottle over the top of Malfoy’s steadying hand and the size difference between their fingers made her heart squeeze.

He dropped to sit next to Hermione on the sofa, kissing away the wetness on Aurora’s cheeks and murmuring low, soothing words against the shell of her ear until slowly, the baby began to calm, her little chest hitching.

The room grew peaceful, the silence broken only by the buzzing of Hermione’s ancient fridgefreezer and Aurora’s enthusiastic gulps as she drained her bottle, her pink socked feet kneading into Hermione’s elbow where they poked over the side of her father’s bicep.

When she’d finished her bottle, Malfoy gently sat Aurora up on his lap, supporting her loosely under her chin with the burp cloth while he rubbed her back in slow circular motions. Now that her tummy was full, the baby seemed quite content, gurgling to herself and grabbing at her feet. She managed to pull one pink sock off in short order, grasping it in her fist and aiming it straight for her mouth.

‘Oh no, socks aren’t for eating, my darling.’ Malfoy smiled affectionately, intercepting the sock from her fist with the air of a man who had done the same thing many times before. He tucked the sock into his pocket and leaned down to pull a small rubber giraffe out from the baby bag instead.

‘Tu veux Sophie la girafe?’ He helped Aurora to coordinate her grip around the chew toy, guiding it carefully to her mouth where she eagerly began gumming down, gripping the giraffe tightly in dribble-soaked fingers.

They both watched her for a moment, Hermione smiling as Aurora grinned at her around her chew toy, Malfoy’s thumbs rubbing gently over Aurora’s leggings as he held her on his lap, resting his chin lightly on the fuzz of her blonde hair and watching the two of them interact.

Finally, Hermione raised her gaze to his, their eyes meeting for the first time since arriving back at the flat. His expression was pained again, almost as if it hurt him to look at her.

‘Draco,’ she began, at the exact same time that Malfoy said….’Granger, I-‘

She waited, looking up at him expectantly, but he seemed to be at a loss as to whatever came next.

‘Umm…coffee?’ She suggested, giving him an out. ‘Or, are you hungry? I could make you a sandwich?’

He grinned, clearly grateful for the reprieve. ‘I’ll never refuse one of your sandwiches, Granger. Not even at three am.’

Hermione was powerless not to smile back, the crinkles at the sides of Malfoy’s eyes slowly becoming her kryptonite.

She left him to change Aurora while she made the sandwiches, and by the time she returned to the sofa, the baby was clean and changed into a set of soft pink, floral print pyjamas.

Malfoy had moved to lie on his back on the floor, holding Aurora above his head and making her squeal with excitement as he lowered her carefully towards his face and blew raspberries onto her belly. Aurora giggled with delight, and it was one of the most beautiful sounds Hermione had ever heard, Malfoy’s answering rumble of laughter threatening to become a close second. She sipped her coffee and watched them together, her chest tight with something she couldn’t define.

Eventually, he sat up, effortlessly transfiguring the car seat into a small crib, and after several minutes of kisses and cuddles, he lay Aurora gently on her back inside. He tucked her blanket tightly around her and then rocked the crib slowly with one foot as he sipped his coffee and ate his sandwich. ‘Jesus Christ, it’s even better than I remember.’ He mumbled, groaning with pleasure after each ravenous bite as though he hadn’t eaten all day. Hermione thought, knowing Malfoy, he probably hadn’t.

She watched him finish up and took his plate, levitating it to the sink as Malfoy let his head drop onto the back of the sofa, his eyes falling closed as a small, satisfied smile spread across his face. Once again, body heat radiated steadily from him, and as she let herself relax against his side, Hermione slowly became aware that the warmth she could feel through his clothes was possibly more body heat than was strictly normal.

Now, with the right lighting, and time to really really look at him, she could see exactly how pale he was, the dark shadows carved deeply under his eyes.

Frowning, she reached a hand up to press the back of it against his forehead. He twitched, and before she could make contact, long, hot fingers were wrapped around her wrist, pulling her hand away. He opened his eyes to appraise her suspiciously.

‘What are you doing, Granger?’

‘You’ve got a temperature.’ Hermione hissed, mortified that she hadn’t noticed sooner. ‘You’re burning up.’

He began pulling away, moving to stand, but Hermione shot out her own hand, curling it in the front of his jumper and pulling him back down. He swore as he unbalanced, thumping onto the sofa next to her.

‘I’m too tired to fight with you again.’ He groaned. ‘It’s nearly four o'clock in the morning. I have to take my daughter home.’

‘You can take Aurora home, Draco.’ She replied evenly. ‘As soon as you’ve let me heal you. That’s the whole reason you came here in the first place isn’t it? It’s practically morning anyway. What difference does it make?’

He scowled. ‘You’re a bloody menace -’

Hermione huffed, rising from the sofa to point a finger at his chest. ‘Stay.’

Malfoy’s eyes went wide, his breath catching in his throat. Hermione hesitated, unsure if she’d gone too far, but he just swallowed thickly and nodded.

She dashed to the cupboard where she’d stored what she thought she’d need to treat the scar before Malfoy had arrived, gathering it quickly and returning to the sofa, half expecting him to have already grabbed Aurora and bolted.

He was still there, watching her with exhausted grey eyes, his expression wary.

Hermione forced herself to swallow a ‘good boy,’ certain that would be a step too far.

He watched silently as she sat down and opened up her medical bag on the small coffee table in front of them, taking out the things she’d prepared earlier - gauze, Dittany, cotton swabs and pain relief potion.

Leaning forwards, he pushed away the vial of pain relief, shaking his head. ‘No potions. Remember, I don’t take anything now I have Aurora.’

Hermione sighed, taking the vial from the table and sliding it back into the medical case without comment. They both knew a simple pain relief potion would do nothing to impair Malfoy’s ability to look after Aurora, but she didn’t want to do or say anything that might spook him.

‘Okay,’ she said quietly. ‘Are you ready? This won’t take long, and then you can go home.’

He sat up straighter, his eyes darting to Aurora, who was sleeping peacefully, the monitoring charms he’d cast glowing brightly above her head.

‘Draco?’

She watched his Adam’s apple bob, his expression reminding her of a rabbit in the headlights. Before he could change his mind, Hermione reached for his hand, sliding her own across the space between them to grasp his fingers.

Malfoy’s response to her touch was immediate, he stilled, his hand closing tightly around hers, eyes snapping to her face. ‘It’s okay,’ she promised. ‘We’ll go slow… I’ll be gentle with you.’ His eyes widened even further than before, the corner of his mouth twitching. Inexplicably though, he seemed to relax a little, blowing out a long breath.

‘Merlin, Granger… a-alright.’

He let go of her hand, reaching over his shoulder to pull his hoodie off in one tug, dragging it over his head and leaving his hair wild as he reappeared in just a soft grey fitted T-shirt. Hermione stiffened as she got her first look at his bare forearm, her stomach lurching violently as the truth of what Malfoy had been hiding came into view.

The soft expanse of his inner arm was mangled, each letter of the scar raw and vicious, much worse than it had been when Bellatrix first carved them into Hermione’s own skin.

His entire forearm was swollen and mottled, red lines of infection bleeding outwards down to his wrist and up to his elbow. She looked up at his face, realising for the first time the true power of Draco Malfoy’s inner strength. It went far beyond what she’d previously thought. He wasn’t just adept at hiding emotional pain. For him to have hidden this level of physical pain for days without potions nor Occlusion… it was just…

Hermione thought back to how he’d handled Aurora with ease, laying her over his arm casually, the way he’d hugged Hermione without flinching. There were no signs to indicate the agony he must have been in. She felt like she was going to cry, again.

Malfoy was watching her, his eyes narrowed. She tried her best to keep her emotions in check, to stop her tears from welling over as she cleared her dry throat. ‘Ridiculous man,’ she muttered and he smirked at that. Stomach swooping, Hermione suddenly found that she had to look away.



* * *



Hermione had healed plenty of people in her life, she’d been in emergency situations, her healing abilities the difference between life and death countless times.

But never had she felt anything even close to what she did now, sitting next to Draco Malfoy on her sofa as he watched her with cool grey eyes and she felt the warmth from his body, the sickly heat radiating from his infected forearm as she wrapped her fingers around his wrist and pulled his hand gently onto her lap.

Usually her patients were in a bed, not sitting opposite her and it made the angle slightly more difficult. Hermione needed to get closer to him in order to get his arm into position and so she shifted forwards as Malfoy turned towards her, bringing one knee up so that she could sit between his legs. She pulled his arm closer, resting it over her thigh. Malfoy had been pliant up to that point, letting her position him however she needed to, but he inhaled sharply as his fingers brushed her lower belly, fidgeting uncomfortably and pulling back a little.

‘Sit still.’ She murmured and he let out a soft laugh.

‘I’m trying to, I’m nervous.’

Hermione laughed too to cover her own terrible nerves. She was painfully aware of how close their faces were, Malfoy’s blonde fringe tickling her face as he bent his head to watch her.

‘I’m going to try and draw some of the infection out first,’ she said, her voice barely above a whisper. ‘It might sting a tiny bit.’ Malfoy nodded, swallowing noisily as his eyes slid up to hers, wide and grey and beautiful.

‘It’s okay,’ he murmured. ‘I trust you.’

Hermione’s stomach plummeted.

The tip of her wand trembled when she first placed it against his skin, terrified to hurt him any more than he must already be hurting.

Malfoy closed his eyes and somehow that was easier.

Hermione closed her own eyes so she didn’t have to look at the mess of his arm as she began to recite the incantation she needed, moving her wand instinctively along the first line in the scrawled letters she knew the exact shape of intimately, without having to see them.

He sucked in a breath as she felt the pull of the spell beginning to work, his fingers flexing against her belly.

Hermione paused, her heart pounding as she looked up at him. He was already trembling, small beads of sweat standing out at his temples. Something was wrong. The spell shouldn’t hurt that much, not straight away and certainly not alongside the pain minimising charm she’d simultaneously cast.

‘Draco?’ She whispered. ‘Are you alright?’

‘Yep,’ he gritted out. ‘It’s just the Black magic being cunty. I’m fine.’ Hermione waited, uncertain.

‘Keep going.’ He urged, and Hermione grit her teeth and nodded. forcing herself to continue moving her wand over the violent slashes of each letter.

D…O...O...L...B

As her wand slowly began trailing over the round curve of the B, Malfoy let out a small whimper, the sound forced through his teeth. Hermione stopped, glancing up at him.

His head was tipped back, the runes below his ear pulled taut over the straining tendons in his throat, his pulse fluttering rapidly.

‘I’m almost done.’ She promised. ‘I’m sorry.’

D….U

He screwed his eyes shut as she began on the M, one tear slowly squeezing out from the corner of his eye and trailing down his cheek. Hermione so desperately wanted to stop and wipe the tear away, but Malfoy needed her to finish. Her scar was poisoning him.

She forced herself to continue, moving her hand up, down, up and finally down again over the M, her wand vibrating with the power of the malevolent, dark magic that was reacting so violently with Malfoy’s blood.

She could feel it fighting her, trying to writhe away from her wand tip and back into his body. Gritting her own teeth, Hermione concentrated her magic, envisioning it pouring into the wound and through Malfoy’s veins, wrapping around the burning heat of Bellatrix’s curse, cool and good and powerful in its own way.

Gently but firmly, Hermione pulled her wand back, pulling the infection with it. One more tug of resistance, and then there was a release, finally some give, as the curse let go of Malfoy’s body and the infection was pulled into her wand.



With a gasp of relief, Hermione dropped the wand onto the sofa, and Malfoy immediately leaned forward, almost bent in half, panting raggedly as he groaned into her lap.

‘I’m so sorry, Draco,’ she whispered, petting over his hair with a trembling hand. ‘I’m sorry. I didn’t know it would hurt so badly.’

‘No,’ he gasped, breathing like he’d run a marathon. ‘It’s good, I feel better. I just - I just need a sec.’

Hermione waited as he panted a moment longer before eventually pushing himself into a slumped sitting position so she could get a good look at his arm.

Already, it appeared so much better, the swelling reduced, the threads of infection pulled back inwards. ‘Bloody hell,’ he murmured, looking down at his arm. ‘You actually did it.’

‘What do you mean?’ Hermione breathed, caught short by the disbelief in his tone. ‘You didn’t think I could?’

He laughed and there was already much more colour in his face, his eyes bright despite the purple bruises from his football tussle still shadowing underneath them. ‘Well-‘ he began. ‘It’s not that exactly… it’s just…I expected the curse to fight you more than it did, that’s all.’ He shook his head, looking down at the soft skin between each letter. ‘I shouldn’t be surprised. You really are incredible in every way, aren’t you?’

Hermione didn’t know what to say to that.

They sat silently for a moment while Malfoy lay back to catch his breath and Hermione forced herself to stop shaking.

‘Right,’ he said eventually, slapping his knees. ‘I really had better get this little lady home.’ He rose to stand, taking one wobbly step before his whole body lurched backwards and he dropped heavily back down onto the sofa, looking surprised.

‘Shit.’ He frowned. ‘Sorry.’ He began pushing down on the cushions so he could attempt to stand again. This time Hermione had to steady him, taking hold of his elbow as he wobbled precariously, almost overbalancing and taking Hermione out with him. ‘For fucks sake.’ He sighed, slumping back down. ‘What's this now?’

‘Draco,’ Hermione said gently. ‘The type of healing you just endured is very draining on your body’s resources. It’s five am. I think you might need to rest before you do anything else.’

He opened bloodshot eyes to look over at Aurora who was dozing peacefully in the cot. ‘I do feel a bit weird…’ he admitted. ’A bit… spinny, like that time I passed out at the hospital.’

Hermione nodded, Malfoy was showing all signs of a magical drain - his body had been subconsciously battling her as the scar resisted her healing magic. Combine that with the fact that he’d had absolutely no sleep for twenty four hours and there was no way he would want to take his precious baby anywhere near a car.

‘Why don’t you stay an hour or two, Draco? Sleep on the sofa.’

He frowned. ‘Really?’

‘Yes. Harry hasn’t come home and it’s nearly morning. I really don’t think you’re safe to be driving yet.’

Malfoy’s eyes were already fighting to close. ‘But…Aurora.’ He murmured groggily.

‘She’s right here.’ Hermione soothed. ‘She’s safe and she’s fast asleep. Just relax. I won’t leave either of you.’

Giving in, he slumped against her, his body going lax. ‘Mmkay, Granger.’ The words were mumbled. ‘Healer’s orders then. If you think... Just.. just five…minutes...’

Hermione smiled, thinking about what Theo had said about Malfoy always doing what she told him to. She patted his arm gently. ‘Good boy.’

He snorted, but relaxed further, dropping his head onto her shoulder. Hermione waited, listening as his breathing began to even out, his body becoming heavier and heavier, his hands unclenching from where they’d been fisted on his thighs.

‘I’ll be back.’ She whispered, lifting his head and wriggling out gingerly from underneath him. She dimmed the lights before leaving to fetch the warm blankets she kept at the end of her bed, quickly stopping off to brush her teeth and change into her own pyjamas.

By the time she returned, Malfoy had turned onto his side, his body curved inwards, his knees bent. One hand lay cushioned beneath his head, the other rested between the bars of Aurora’s crib, which he’d pulled closer to the sofa so that his face was level with hers.



Aurora had also turned onto her side, one hand curled tightly around her father’s index finger. Both Malfoys were fast asleep, breathing peacefully in tandem.

The pang in Hermione’s chest was painful.

With a wistful sigh she very gently tucked Aurora’s blanket under her arms and then, unable to stop herself, she bent and carefully smoothed Draco’s hair away from his face, planting a soft, tender kiss on his cheek.

He stirred. ‘Granger?’

Hermione froze, waiting. He went silent for so long that she thought she must have imagined it. She turned and began padding towards her bedroom.

‘I meant what I said, you know.’

She turned. Malfoy’s eyes were still shut, the words mumbled so that Hermione had to move back towards him so she could hear. ‘What did you mean, Draco?’ She whispered.

‘Krum…’ The name was so quiet she had to bend over him, her hair falling in a curtain over his face. ‘If he ever does anything to hurt you… I’ll fucking kill him.’

Hermione’s heart beat so loudly in her chest that she was surprised Malfoy couldn’t hear it. She blinked into the darkness, her hands gripping the arm of the sofa tightly.

‘Draco,’ she whispered. ‘Viktor… We aren’t… he’s not...’

But Malfoy couldn’t hear her. He’d fallen fast asleep, his breathing slow and even as his body relaxed and his mouth grew soft, his bottom lip pouting gently in an exact mirror of the beautiful baby girl sleeping next to him.

Hermione stepped back, her pulse fluttering frantically. Why did it feel so important that Malfoy knew she wasn’t with Viktor? What did she expect him to do with that information?

She looked down at him once more, at the pale fall of blonde hair spread over the cushion, the long lashes splayed over pale cheeks, then down to the hand his daughter held, his platinum wedding band gleaming in the moonlight.

Hermione shook her head, forcing herself to turn back towards her bedroom.

No matter what she was beginning to feel, whatever this inexplicable pull was between them, Draco Malfoy was not someone she could let herself want.

Because, as long as he wore that ring on his finger, Malfoy still belonged to Astoria, whether he knew it or not.



Chapter End Notes

Chapter 18: Should tumble and fall

See the end of the chapter for more notes



The weeks after the visit to Narcissa quickly evolved into a routine.

Evidently, Theo and Harry’s first date had gone well because afterwards they became inseparable - any time they weren’t at work they were together, out on dates or going between the flat and the Penthouse.

As a result, Hermione spent more time than ever with Malfoy and Aurora and when they came back from Italy, Blaise and Pansy too.

Pansy was particularly pleased with the development, squealing with happiness when she and Blaise returned home and she discovered what had happened in their three- week absence.

‘Finally!’ She’d gushed, kissing Harry on both cheeks. ‘Prepare yourself to be worshipped, darling. Our Theo is an incorrigible romantic.’

‘He’s a horny git.’ Malfoy had muttered.

‘That too.’ Pansy’s expression was that of a proud mother. ‘You’re a lucky man, Harry Potter.’

Blaise had taken the news a little more in his stride, giving only a knowing smirk as he shook Harry’s hand. ‘Another Gryffindor in the circle of trust.’ He mused. ‘Who would have thought it?’

When Harry and Theo weren’t busy mauling each other, they hosted drinks nights and dinners. Harry cooked while Theo manhandled him, glued to his side under the pretence of learning, although anyone with a pair of eyes could see that Theo was far more of a hindrance than a help.

The new couple seemed content to endure company while they ate, but as the night progressed, they would inevitably end up entangled on the sofa, their tongues in each other’s mouths, slowly becoming less and less aware that they were in a room full of people.

Hermione found that she had never been so grateful for Muffliatos and Notice-me-nots. She actually thought Malfoy was going to cry the first time he overheard Theo whispering in Harry’s ear about their plans for later that night. He had been sitting next to them on the sofa, his eyes slowly widening in shock as the men on his other side began laughing quietly before apparently attempting to devour each other right there in the living area. He’d stared over at Hermione helplessly as she giggled and invited him back to the flat for coffee instead.

Malfoy had risen from the sofa, scooping Aurora from her playmat and Accioing his coat.

‘You two,’ he’d muttered grimly, pointing between Harry and Theo. ‘Are filthy. Never in my life have I heard anything so…’ he’d shaken his head, blinking slowly in disbelief. ‘And in the presence of my daughter, too.’

Theo had laughed out loud as Harry failed to hide a smile behind his hand. ‘She couldn’t hear us,’ Theo shrugged. ‘And she wouldn’t have understood even if she did, she’s not even six months old, Malfoy.’

Malfoy had clutched Aurora to his chest, pressing their cheeks together. ‘She’s very intelligent.’ He’d muttered seriously as Aurora grinned at Theo, who was pulling faces at her from the couch. ‘She picks up on more than you’d think. And anyway, I could hear you, and I am traumatised.’

After that, Hermione often had Malfoy and Aurora spending hours at hers, Malfoy’s guilt over invading Theo’s space seemingly superseding his guilt about turning up at her flat unannounced. He always brought flowers or Percy Pigs or a bottle of her favourite wine, and Hermione, who had inadvertently started waiting hopefully for a pull on the wards, did her best to control her thrill of anticipation any time she opened the door to find him standing there.

Thankfully, since the night he’d broken down, Malfoy seemed to have turned a corner. He immersed himself in fatherhood, his relationship with Aurora going from strength to strength. He pored over the books Hermione brought him on baby development, tracking each one of Aurora’s milestones with fervour, taking endless pictures for her album, his joy evident as he annotated each photo and affixed it to the extra pages he charmed so that he could fit them all in.

He continued visiting his mother regularly, and appeared to have finally committed to therapy, coming back each time pale-faced and grumpy, his hands fisted at his sides. After half an hour or so, he would allow Hermione or Pansy to give him a hug and then sit murmuring quietly to Aurora until he came around.

Hermione insisted on checking Malfoy’s scar often, ensuring that it continued to heal. Every time he allowed her to look, it had improved. The raw skin had gradually become paler, the slashes less violent, the hateful letters slowly sealing themselves under his skin, irrevocable evidence of the bond that Malfoy’s actions had forged between them.

Hermione felt that bond like a physical thing, forcing herself to ignore the ever-present ache in her chest, the tether that seemed to pull her thoughts directly to wherever Draco Malfoy might be at any given time, day or night. Try as she might, Hermione couldn’t forget the way he’d looked at her as she healed him that first night, the colour of his eyes up close, how he’d shivered when she’d told him she’d be gentle.

But Malfoy never mentioned what she’d heard the night they’d visited Narcissa, and he didn't bring up Viktor again. Hermione didn’t know how to tell him she wasn’t interested in pursuing things with Viktor without making it obvious how much she wanted him to know, and so the words remained stuck in her throat, clogging her airways, growing harder and harder to vocalise the longer time went on.



* * *



May rolled in, warm and welcoming after a particularly cold April.

That Sunday morning Hermione had been working in the kitchen of the flat, completing the final edits of her research project, when she felt a gentle pull on the wards indicating what she had come to recognise as Malfoy’s familiar magical signature.

She quickly rolled up the parchment she’d been working on and stashed it out of sight, running her fingers through her hair as she padded to the door.

Malfoy stood in the hallway, levitating two takeaway coffees out in front of him, one hand protectively cradling Aurora’s head where she was nestled in her carrier, fast asleep on his chest.

He looked casual yet polished in a navy collared T-shirt and dark trousers, his hair still damp from the shower and swept back from his face, immaculate, other than the scar on his arm and a nasty red scuff mark that swelled beneath his left eye.

‘Draco,’ Hermione sighed. ‘Seriously, are you ever going to get through a single football match without finding a reason to fight?’

He grinned, stepping towards her and bending to give her a kiss on the cheek.

‘The other guy started it.’ He said, closing his eyes and smirking as he allowed Hermione to reach up and stroke her fingers gently over the tender skin below his eye. She huffed, turning to fetch the dittany and cotton wool from where she kept them within easy reach in her kitchen cupboard.

He waited patiently as Hermione poured some ointment onto a cotton ball and dabbed it carefully over his tender cheekbone. ‘This is supposed to be my day off,’ she tutted, grasping his chin gently to tilt his head left and right, checking for any more injuries. ‘I didn’t actually sign up to be your personal Healer-on-demand, Draco.’

Grey eyes flashed open and a wicked smile curved one side of his mouth, even as he attempted to look contrite.

‘Thug.’ She grumbled, doing her best not to smile back even as her traitorous heart skipped a beat. This time, Malfoy laughed out loud, giving up any pretence of remorse.

‘It’s stress relief.’ He flicked a hand to set the coffees down on her kitchen counter. ‘Everyone needs a way to blow off steam.’

Hermione shook her head. ‘I’m sure you can think of a better way.’ She said, blushing when Malfoy’s eyes snapped to hers and then immediately flicked away as their gaze unexpectedly met. She watched his Adam’s apple bob and stepped back, suddenly aware of how close they were standing.

‘How’s the presentation going?’ He asked, honing in on the biscuit tin she’d fetched and now offered him, reaching inside and predictably coming out with two custard creams (Hermione absolutely did not buy them specifically because they were Malfoy’s favourite).

‘It’s mostly done, other than a few minor edits.’

‘You know the offer still stands if you need someone to check it over. I’m more than happy to take a look.’ Malfoy took a bite of his biscuit.

‘No!’ The word tumbled out a little too quickly.

‘Thanks, but I don’t think I can look at it again without going mad.’

He held her eye for a moment, chewing slowly, a tiny frown creasing between his brows as he swallowed. ‘Alright. But I’m always here if you change your mind. It’s due on Wednesday?’

Hermione nodded, once again assaulted by the familiar rush of anxiety that hit her every time she thought about presenting her proposal.

She’d worked hard on it, spent weeks going over her research and her budgeting, ensuring her numbers were sound. She’d collated a huge amount of evidence from the Muggle world to back up her projections for a magical equivalent, had pored over every bit of scholarly information she could access, confident that she would be able to answer any question the board threw at her, and positive she could prove that what she was proposing would make a significant difference to Magical maternity care. But she would need a lot to make it happen, money, staff, time. She didn’t know if St Mungo’s even had the resources she would require in order to go ahead with her project. She worried she’d been too ambitious before she’d even gotten out of the starting gate.

‘Granger,’ Malfoy’s voice pulled her out of her spiral. ‘You can do this, alright? I’ve seen how hard you’ve worked the last few weeks, and I know that your presentation will be better than anything they’ve seen at St Mungo’s before. You’re Hermione bloody Granger for fucks sake, they’re lucky to have you even working there. Don’t doubt yourself, even for a second.’

Hermione’s throat tightened. Malfoy sounded so unbelievably genuine. He’d said the words with such conviction that she couldn’t bring herself to voice her doubts aloud, especially not when he was looking at her like that - as though he so badly wanted her to succeed, even though he had no idea what her presentation was about.

‘We’ll find out on Wednesday, I suppose.’ She said instead, forcing a lightness into her voice.

‘Also, not that I mind, but why are you here so early? You’re still coming out for lunch later, aren’t you?’

Malfoy's face darkened almost comically, and Hermione had to hold back a smile.

The week before, Harry had made the huge step of inviting Theo out for Sunday dinner with their friends, something Hermione had planned weeks ago to coincide with Ginny’s next visit home.

Theo had looked suitably horrified for a beat before the gravity of the gesture sank in, and then he’d flushed uncharacteristically, smiling against the pleased curve of Harry’s mouth when he’d pulled him in for a passionate kiss.

Theo obviously wanted to go, but had insisted on Malfoy coming too, adamant he needed emotional support if he was going to be spending the afternoon in a pub full of Gryffindors.

‘Of course,’ Harry’d agreed. ‘Malfoy was always invited. Pansy and Blaise also, if they’re free.’

Malfoy had baulked violently at the suggestion, but he’d inevitably succumbed to Theo’s pleas, unable to refuse the ‘you know I’d do it for you,’ card. He’d relented reluctantly, however, grumbling something about being stupid enough to voluntarily enter the lion's den.

Delighted, Theo had grabbed Malfoy’s face and kissed him too, a brief but enthusiastic peck on the lips that had Malfoy cackling with surprise and Harry yanking Theo back by the waist, eyes flashing with barely suppressed possessiveness even as he’d laughed along with Malfoy.

‘Don’t be jealous, Harry,’ Theo had turned in Harry’s arms, smirking. ‘And don’t think that’s the first time we’ve kissed, either. How do you suppose I perfected my excellent kissing technique? Draco had a positively sinful mouth before he was betrothed, perfect for practising on.’

Harry’s expression had matched exactly how Hermione felt at the thought of Theo and Malfoy learning how to kiss by kissing each other whilst at Hogwarts - shocked, intrigued and also… yes, definitely a little envious too.

She’d looked at Malfoy, who had turned far too pink for Theo to be lying. ‘Like Nott said,’ he’d shrugged. ‘We were inexperienced, ridiculously sheltered teenage boys, it was solely for research purposes, making sure we were good kissers before unleashing ourselves on anyone else.’

Heat flushed low in Hermione’s belly at the confirmation, and Harry looked about set to combust.

‘It hasn’t… you’re not… you don’t like men, though, do you, Malfoy? I didn’t think…’

Both Malfoy and Theo had laughed at that.

‘No, Potter,’ Malfoy drawled, but he’d been looking at Hermione when he continued. ‘I’m definitely, definitely into women. I was being a good friend, that’s all.’

‘Fuck.’ Harry had scrubbed a hand over his face as he’d looked between the two men, his pupils like pennies. ‘That’s… fuck…’

A moment later, he’d dragged Theo desperately down the hallway to his bedroom, shoving a smirking Theo into the room and slamming the door so hard behind them that it juddered on its hinges.

And so, plans had been made.

This Sunday was to be the official blending of the friendship groups. Malfoy, Aurora and Theo were meeting Hermione and Harry at the Leaky Cauldron at one, along with Ron, Padma and their children, Luna and her son, Atlas, Seamus and Dean, Neville and Hannah. Pansy and Blaise were going to try to come later, unable to get out of a prior engagement.

All week, Hermione had been anticipating the lunch, nervous about how Harry would manage to mix two groups of people with such different backgrounds and such fraught history.

‘We’re still coming,’ Malfoy said now, unclipping Aurora, who had awoken and was wriggling impatiently in her baby carrier. ‘Theo would kill me if I didn’t.’

Hermione stepped forward and helped him wrangle Aurora out, giving the baby a little kiss on the top of her head as she greeted her. She looked adorable in a set of soft baby blue leggings and a collared white top, the scalloped edges of the collar sewn with matching blue thread. Aurora’s socks were white today, little frilly ankle socks with pale blue bows affixed to the sides.

Hermione wondered again if it was Malfoy who chose Aurora’s clothes or if Pansy did all her shopping. The baby was always so impeccably dressed, her outfits always perfectly matched, whilst always being cosy and comfortable enough for her delicate baby skin. Somehow, she had a feeling it was him.

Malfoy pulled the blanket from the back of the couch and spread it out on the floor, taking Aurora back and sitting down, propping her in a seated position between his spread legs. Aurora immediately leaned forward, making a grab for Sophie La Girafe, whom Malfoy had produced from his pocket and held out in front of her, encouraging her to reach for it.

‘She’s so close to sitting unaided,’ Hermione marvelled. ‘I can’t believe how quickly she’s growing up.’

‘Don’t.’ He muttered miserably, leaning back to allow Aurora to support herself whilst remaining just behind her - a safe place for his little girl to land if she did happen to fall. His expression was pained as he dropped a kiss into her white blonde fluff.

‘I need her to slow down. Before long, she’ll be ready to disappear off to Hogwarts and I’ll still be wanting to spend every second with her, not ready to let her out of my sight.’ He looked distraught, and the ache in Hermione’s chest intensified in sympathy.

She took a final sip of her coffee and then moved to sit opposite him on the floor, crossing her legs and smiling at Aurora, using one finger to tickle the sole of the baby’s foot. Aurora giggled and reached towards her. Hermione moved closer and lowered herself to her elbows, allowing Aurora’s little hands to explore her face.

‘Ah, I actually just wanted to talk to you about something.’ Malfoy said from above, sounding hesitant. Hermione peeked up at him, her attention caught by the seriousness in his tone.

‘Are you okay?’ Hermione took hold of Aurora’s hands, clapping them gently together, distracting her from trying to jab a tiny finger up her nose.

Malfoy looked agonised, chewing the inside of his cheek. ‘I’m okay…it’s just that I’ve made rather a big decision, and I wanted to tell someone, I guess...talking to the Mind Healer has confirmed that I have to do this if I ever want to have any hope of moving on.’

Hermione’s pulse sped up immediately, her eyes darting down to where Malfoy’s hand twitched on his thigh, ready to make a grab for Aurora if she toppled. His wedding ring was still there, shining from its usual place on the third finger of his left hand.

‘Okay…’ she said slowly, giving him time to say whatever it was that had him so anxious.

He moved on to biting his lower lip, still apparently trying to figure out how to tell her.

Hermione waited, used to his long silences after six months of friendship. Malfoy had a habit of sorting slowly through his thoughts before vocalising them, always so careful with his words.

‘I’ve decided to sell the manor.’ He said eventually, appearing relieved to have just come out with it.

Although that had been the last thing Hermione had been expecting him to say, she found herself deflating slightly before the weight of his meaning actually hit her.

‘Wait,’ she held up a hand. ‘You’re planning to sell your ancestral family home? A property that’s been passed through your bloodline for generations. The place you were born and have lived in your entire life?’

‘Yes… well, I was planning to auction it, actually. Donate the money to charity.’ Malfoy sighed, shaking his head. ‘I can’t… I can’t live there again, Granger. I can't raise Aurora in a house where Voldemort murdered people in the dining room, where you were tortured and my mother was…’ he cut himself off, taking in a shaking breath. ‘Where my father hung himself, and where Astoria…’ his eyes suddenly glistened, his voice tight with emotion. ‘I can’t.’ He said quietly. ‘I keep picturing Astoria there alone, scared and likely bleeding, knowing that she was either about to lose our baby or die herself. She must have been so terrified, and I wasn’t there. It fucking kills me.’

He was panting a little, his chest hitching like he was about to descend into a panic attack.

‘Draco,’ Hermione put a hand on his arm, squeezing in an attempt to ground him. ‘It’s okay, love. Breathe.’

He put his own hand over hers, gripping it tightly as he inhaled deeply several times, getting himself back under control.

‘So many people I love have suffered in that house, Granger. So many people I either couldn’t or didn’t help. I can’t stand it. Any time I’ve had to go back there, it’s like the walls are closing in on me, worse than when I was in Azkaban.

I need something good to come from the manor’s existence. I think that the amount of land alone could raise good galleons, even if they burned the fucking building to the ground.’

He finally stopped, taking in a ragged breath as he watched her face with dark, anxious eyes, seemingly waiting for her to speak.

‘Draco… I understand,’ Hermione began. ‘I think that’s an excellent way for you to make a fresh start…’ She paused, unable to continue without asking. ‘But how will you physically untether yourself from the manor? How will you make it safe for another family to potentially live there? I’m sorry, but it’s well known there’s a lot of dark magic tied into the foundations of that house, I don’t think it will let you go that easily.’

He sagged at her words, resignation stealing the light from his eyes. ‘You’re right, and I don’t actually know, yet. I haven’t gotten that far. I’m waiting on Blaise to come back to me on the legal implications first, what kind of paperwork it will entail to remove myself from the deeds, etcetera. In terms of dark magic…I need to build up my tolerance for being at the manor first, spend more time there so I can figure out what kind of spells tie my blood to the place and if I can break them.’ He nodded down at Hermione’s bare forearm. ‘I’ve done it once, who’s to say I can’t do it again?’

Hermione stared at him in disbelief. Between them, an oblivious Aurora babbled happily, chewing on the head of her giraffe. One of her socks had come off, and Malfoy distractedly picked it up, pushing it into his pocket. ‘Have you forgotten how badly my scar hurt you, Draco?’ Hermione gasped. ‘The magic I pulled from your arm was dark, it was angry that you’d taken it from its intended target. Do you really think Malfoy Manor is going to just give up the last of its bloodline just like that? You won’t even have a glass of wine in case it impairs your ability to look after Aurora or your mum, but you’ll gladly put yourself at risk of ancient curses, again?’

Hermione forced herself to soften her voice as Malfoy flinched, already pulling away. She tightened her fingers around his, preventing him from letting go.

‘Granger,’ he said, his voice low, his eyes hard as they held her gaze, unwavering. ‘Everything I do is for the people I love. Everything.’

‘I know.’ The words came out barely above a whisper, her chest constricted by the weight of Malfoy’s words.

‘I’m doing this for Aurora. I don’t want her to be tied to the manor the way I have been. I want to give her a fresh start, somewhere safe and happy,’ he gestured around Hermione’s small flat. ‘Somewhere like this. I love it here. I feel the warmth of this place pouring through my veins whenever I cross the wards, I want this for Aurora. I want...’ he cut himself off, closing his eyes as he bit back whatever he’d been going to say.

Hermione swallowed past the lump in her throat as she thought back to the pompous teenage boy who had strutted around Hogwarts so many years ago, bragging about his blood status, his wealth, his family's power.

The man in front of her was so far away from those things, so full of love, so selfless, so kind - he was so beautiful it hurt Hermione to look at him.

Malfoy opened his eyes again slowly, fixing them on her face, as though her opinion meant something, as though he needed her blessing even though she was no one important in the scheme of his life, not really.

‘Just promise me you’ll do it properly,’ she said. ‘Get help from the Aurors. Promise me you won’t get hurt, Draco. Your family needs you, your friends need you. I n-‘

‘You what, Granger?’ He demanded quietly, suddenly leaning forward so their faces were just inches apart, his grey eyes searching hers over the top of Aurora's head. ‘What do you even-‘

‘Hey, fuckers!’

The sound of the door banging open made them both jump, springing apart.

Harry paused in the doorway, clearly sensing that he and Theo had interrupted something, but Theo had spotted Aurora on the floor and paid Hermione and Malfoy no mind, striding across the room and diving flat onto his belly next to her.

‘Roary! Where’ve you been?’ He asked, pulling Aurora towards him. ‘Uncle Theo missed you after football.’

Aurora shrieked with excitement, giggling uncontrollably as Theo pretended to be some sort of animal, digging his face into her soft belly to make her squirm.

Hermione scrambled to stand, moving quickly out of the way of Theo’s long legs. She greeted Harry with a hug, still not quite able to look at Malfoy, her heart still fluttering after the tension of the minutes before.

From his place on the blanket, Theo suddenly yelped in pain. ‘Ahh, Malfoy!’ He called. ‘She’s got my hair. Get her off… ‘ he twisted on the ground.

Aurora had clearly and predictably made the most of the unrestricted access she’d had to Theo’s head and had managed to tangle her little fingers in his curls, his dark hair wrapped firmly around both hands.

‘Malfoy!’ Theo called again, giving a pained-sounding laugh as Aurora continued enthusiastically pulling on large fistfuls of his hair. ‘Call off your daughter. She’s hurting me!’

‘No can do, sorry, Nott.’ Malfoy smirked, sitting back and making no attempt to help Theo extricate himself from Aurora’s sticky grip. ‘I’ve been training her up. She’s in attack mode now.’

Theo laughed nervously again, his eyes squeezed shut as he reached up blindly to try and free himself. ‘Congrats. She’s bloody lethal.’ He gritted out as above him, Aurora squealed with joy, thoroughly enjoying the game.

‘Come on, babe.’ Harry laughed from next to her. ‘You’re getting your arse handed to you by a six-month-old.’

Theo opened one blue eye, raising a hand to give Harry the finger. ‘Thanks for the moral support, babe.’ He panted, and Hermione giggled.

With a sigh, Malfoy eventually gave in, reaching for Aurora and carefully untangling her fingers from Theo’s hair, pulling several brown strands out from the tight circle of her chubby fist.

‘Be gentle, my darling.’ He said quietly. ‘We must have kind hands, like this, see?’ He gently directed Aurora’s hand to his own face, guiding her to stroke her little palm over his cheek, his large hand completely enveloping her smaller one. Aurora thought this was also hilarious, and she giggled as Malfoy laughed too, stroking her hand over his cheek and repeating, ‘kind hands, kind hands, okay?’ He smirked. ‘Unless it’s a boy and then you have daddy’s permission to hex his balls off.’

‘Uh, Malfoy.’ Harry said from the kitchen. ‘I thought we weren’t allowed to use the word balls around Aurora?’ He smirked. ‘You told Theo and me off last time.’

Without missing a beat, Malfoy sent a wandless stinging jinx at Harry, who dodged it and laughed.

Theo sat up, looking dishevelled, his hair sticking up where Aurora had yanked it. He rubbed the top of his head, grumbling. ‘Merlin, she’s got a good grip for a little one.’

He stood up and reached down, taking Aurora from Malfoy and nestling her in his arms. ‘Good skills, Babygirl.’ He murmured. ‘You’re tough, aren’t you, Roary?’

‘Don’t call her that.’ Malfoy dusted himself off as he also rose to stand. ‘You know I hate it.’

‘It’s cute.’ Theo argued. ‘Plus, it suits her when she’s in Malfoy mode.’ Malfoy said nothing, simply raising one blonde eyebrow.

‘Is everyone almost ready to go?’ Harry asked, suddenly looking a little nervous. ‘Lunch is booked in half an hour and it’s a ten-minute walk from here.’

Hermione gasped, realising the time. ‘I just need to jump in the shower,’ she said. ‘I won’t be long.’

She left the three men and Aurora in the sitting area while she grabbed a towel and her clothes, disappearing into the bathroom to get ready.

She washed her hair quickly, her mind working over the conversation with Malfoy as she rinsed and conditioned, trying and failing to predict where it might have taken them if they hadn’t been interrupted.

Hermione dried herself off magically, squeezing as much moisture out of her hair as she could before adding in some curl-defining cream and going through her basic make-up routine, adding an extra slick of mascara and some lip gloss.

She’d gone for a sundress today, with the weather being unseasonably warm. It meant her arms were bare and that her friends were going to see her unblemished skin, as well as the scar on Malfoy’s forearm for the first time.

She’d told Padma, Ginny and Luna about her complex friendship with Malfoy weeks ago, trusting the information to filter through to the men. She’d explained carefully how they had grown closer after she’d supported him through Aurora’s birth and the aftermath of Astoria’s death. She’d explained his reasons for taking her scar, told them how much he’d changed in the years since the war.

The reaction had been mixed, everyone but Luna reacting with disbelief that Hermione was so willing to forgive Malfoy for their past and that he had changed so much that he’d be willing to do something so personally detrimental. It had taken her several hours to explain their friendship, longer for her friends to accept it, but ultimately, they all had their own lives, and Hermione was thirty years old. Her friends had the choice to either accept that Malfoy and Theo were now part of their group, or to push Harry and Hermione away. Thankfully, the unanimous decision seemed to be that life had moved on over the past decade, and so could they.

However, she was nervous about how the group would react to actually seeing her scar on Malfoy’s arm, knowing it was bound to bring up some difficult emotions for everyone. She hoped, for Harry’s sake, that both sides were able to forgive if not completely forget.

Hermione paused to look at herself in the mirror before leaving the bathroom. She eyed herself critically, smoothing down the pale yellow fabric of her dress, adjusting the thin shoulder straps and fiddling with the bow at the back that tied it in place.

Finally, having run out of time, she left the bathroom to rejoin Harry, Theo and Malfoy in the lounge.





* * *



‘Bloody hell, Granger.’ Theo blurted as she stepped out of the hallway, disentangling himself from Harry’s arms long enough to give a low, appreciative whistle. Harry smiled, untucking his face from Theo’s neck, green eyes soft. ‘Beautiful as always, Mione.’

Hermione looked down, confused. She hadn’t thought the outfit was anything special, it was a simple sundress, cut to the knee, nothing scandalous. She’d only chosen it because she liked the yellow shade against her olive skin.

Malfoy was on the floor, apparently just having finished changing Aurora. He was rising to stand when Theo whistled, glancing over to Hermione and suddenly going very still.

Hermione felt rooted to the spot as his grey eyes travelled over her from head to toe, slowly, as though he couldn’t look away. He blinked hazily, his gaze trailing over her damp curls and down, dipping into the slight swell of her breasts before following the curve of her waist and then sweeping down her bare legs and up again, meeting her eyes briefly before flicking quickly away. His lips parted slightly as if he was going to say something, but no sound came out. Hermione swallowed heavily, her tongue darting out to self-consciously lick over her glossed lips. Malfoy followed the movement of her tongue, a muscle ticking in his jaw, his nostrils flaring.

Behind them, Harry cleared his throat ‘Are we ready?’ He asked, fiddling with the collar of his shirt and running a hand anxiously through his hair.

From his side, Theo’s blue eyes flashed knowingly as he raised his eyebrows at Malfoy. ‘She looks incredible, doesn’t she, Draco?’

There was a long pause, and Hermione felt her cheeks flush, embarrassed by the unexpected attention. ‘Uh, yes.’ Malfoy’s voice came out a little hoarse. ‘You look… erm, lovely, Granger.’

Theo rolled his eyes, apparently dissatisfied with Malfoy’s response. Harry didn’t seem to have noticed. He was uncharacteristically quiet, distracted, even as Theo reached a hand up to his cheek and cupped his jaw to kiss him softly on the lips. ‘Are you okay?’ He asked, and Harry nodded, worrying at his lower lip.

‘I’m good. I just don’t want to be late, let’s go.’

Malfoy was still standing motionless on the blanket when Hermione turned back to him.

‘Draco?’ She called. ‘Are you coming?’ He blinked, grey eyes slowly coming back into focus, clutching Aurora tighter to his chest.

‘Yes,’ he muttered. ‘I’m.. yes. I’ll be right there.’



* * *



They left the flat a few minutes later, stopping briefly so Malfoy could grab Aurora’s pram from the car and load it up with everything he thought he’d need to get her through lunch. He tucked a blanket around the dozing baby, smoothing her hair back from her face. ‘Let’s do this, then.’ He muttered, waiting for Harry and Theo to walk before grasping onto the handle and falling into step next to Hermione.

The four of them meandered down the street together, making their way to the pub.

In front of Hermione and Malfoy, Harry and Theo held hands, walking silently. Theo’s eyes darted to look at Harry every few minutes, but Harry was quiet, his usual easy manner replaced by tense shoulders and a tight jaw. He smiled when Theo whispered something in his ear, squeezing his hand tighter as they got closer to the pub.

Hermione chattered aimlessly to a mostly silent Malfoy, who also seemed deep in thought, his own reticence obvious. ‘This feels unwise, Granger,’ he murmured as they approached the pub. ‘I can’t believe I’m actually doing this of my own volition.’

‘You’re doing this because you’re a good friend,’ Hermione smiled. ‘I think this means a lot to Theo.’

Malfoy nodded gravely. ‘I know it does. I’ve never seen him this happy with anyone.’

‘Lunch is going to be fine,’ Hermione said decisively, more for her own benefit than his. ‘It’s just Sunday dinner. How much drama can there be?’

‘Exactly,’ Theo said, turning around from in front of them. ‘It’s going to be great. We’re all going to have a wonderful time.’

Harry and Malfoy said nothing, their eyes meeting and holding as a skeptical look passed between them. ‘I’m sure you’re right, Theo,’ Malfoy agreed. ‘We’ll all be holding hands and singing by the time they bring dessert.’



* * *



To begin with, as they entered the pub, Hermione really thought Theo was right and the lunch was going to be far less awkward than she’d expected.

Her heart warmed when she saw her friends already sitting around the table, some of them with drinks, others with menus open in front of them. Ginny was deep in conversation with Dean and Seamus, motioning enthusiastically with her hand as she told them a story. Luna and Padma sat together, bouncing Fred and Atlas on their laps and chatting. Neville sat straight-backed next to Hannah, his pint pausing halfway to his mouth when he clocked them coming in, blue eyes fixing on Malfoy.

‘Oh, you’re here!’ Ginny called, breaking away from her conversation when she noticed them too. ‘Hi! Come and sit down.’

En masse, the group rose from the table to greet them, a flurry of shaken hands, hugs and air kisses ensuing.

Theo greeted each person warmly and enthusiastically, Harry’s hand remaining firmly at the small of his back as he made his way around the table, a small proprietary smile on Harry’s face as he watched Theo interact so naturally with his friends.

Malfoy hung back, loitering behind Hermione and gripping the handle of Aurora’s pram tightly with one hand. He smiled stiffly as various people politely shook his hand, peering in to smile at his sleeping baby.

Ginny was the last to approach, her eyes shining. ‘Hey, Ferret,’ she grinned. ‘Long time no see.’

‘Weaslette,’ Malfoy replied. ‘How’s life treating you with the Harpies? You killed that last match.’ Taken by surprise, Ginny only paused for a beat, and within seconds, she and Malfoy had entered into conversation, all of Malfoy’s awkwardness seeming to disappear as the two of them talked enthusiastically about Quidditch.

After a few minutes, the group sat back down. Malfoy pulled up a chair next to Hermione and dropped into it, sighing with relief. He was quiet as the conversation restarted, rocking Aurora’s pram gently, grey eyes warily surveying the table.

Hermione picked up a menu and began to scan the different options, although she couldn’t really see them, too focused on watching Neville, who was still watching Malfoy.

‘Where’s Ron?’ Harry asked, spotting the empty space next to Padma.

‘He’s at the bar,’ Ginny turned to motion at the empty chair on Hermione’s other side. ‘Oh, and there’s a surprise too. I bumped into someone at a game yesterday, and he had today off from training, so I invited him out with us for lunch.’ She raised her eyebrows at Hermione before jerking her head behind her with a wry grin. ‘Luckily, he has unrestricted access to an international Portkey.’

Hermione turned to look, and at her side, Malfoy did the same, swearing quietly under his breath. ‘For fucks sake,’ he murmured. ‘You’ve got to be fucking kidding me.’

Hermione’s heart seemed to have stopped in her chest, her stomach lurching so dramatically she thought she might vomit there and then.

‘Herminny!’ Viktor grinned, approaching the table with two pints in his hand, a flushed and obviously starstruck Ron at his side. ‘I hope you don’t mind, I wanted to surprise you.’



Chapter End Notes

Hermione girl- time to be honest with yourself!
