Chapter 19: All the mountains


Chapter Notes

Helllooooo! Firstly! Sorry I kept you all hanging on after last chapter’s cliffie! Please take this longer chapter as an apology. Also, I feel I need to preface this one with some warnings/ reminders.

The people in this story are flawed. None of them are perfect and none of them can act in a way that pleases everyone all the time.

Hermione is tactless in this chapter. She knows it and she owns it. She’s a good person but ultimately, she’s falling hard and can’t always do the right thing, as much as she always wants to.

I also want to make some excuses for Neville here and hope that there’s no outright bashing of him, despite his behaviour. Again, he’s flawed and he’s acting off his emotions (and alcohol) but ultimately, he’s just hurting and hurt people lash out.

A lot happens this chapter and once again I’ve agonised over it obsessively, really hoping I’ve got it right! Thanks to Didsomeonesay_dracomalfoy, Black_phoenix22 and Smudgesonapage for their patience and support while I’ve had yet another meltdown! (she’s dramatic)And thank you all for reading and sticking with me!

Edited to add, the comment about Ron’s eyes was inspired by a fabulous piece of Drarry art I saw by Eleadore. It made me cackle!



See the end of the chapter for more notes



Hermione stood up, not knowing what to do other than to wait as Viktor put the drinks down on the table and made a beeline towards her.

‘Hello,’ he beamed, stepping closer and embracing her in a tight, warm hug. ‘I missed you.’

Hermione couldn’t move, she could barely breathe as Viktor’s strong arms pinned her own to her sides, and the hug went on for an uncomfortably long time.

She was painfully aware that most of the restaurant's attention was on them, and her face, despite being squashed into Viktor’s broad chest, burned hot with familiar embarrassment.

‘How have you been?’ Viktor said as he eventually released her, stepping back to look her up and down. ‘You look absolutely stunning, Herminny. Yellow suits you.’

Across the table, where he and Harry were currently watching with rabid interest, Hermione caught Theo in her peripheral. He huffed, throwing his hands up and widening his eyes meaningfully in Malfoy’s direction.

Although she couldn’t see him, Hermione could feel Malfoy behind her. His magic was seeping out into the room, tension rolling off him in waves.

‘Th-thank you, Viktor. I’ve been good.’ Hermione replied quietly. ‘You look well too.’

Viktor did look well. He’d been training in France and evidently, the weather had been agreeable. He was noticeably tanned, the bronze of his skin set off by the white linen shirt he wore. He smiled down at her, dark hair falling into even darker eyes, his Bulgarian heritage brought to the fore in a most striking way.

Objectively, Hermione knew she should feel something when she looked at Viktor, that everyone else in the room saw something she didn’t - she couldn’t. Because as handsome as Viktor was - as kind, as talented, as desirable - he just ….wasn’t…

Finally, Viktor released her, and Hermione plopped back into her seat, raising her eyes to see the entire table pretend they hadn’t been watching and suddenly reanimate, a low buzz of conversation resuming, overly polite smiles pasted onto almost every face.

Ron had made his way reluctantly towards Theo and Harry, and Hermione felt a swell of love for Theo as she watched him jump up to greet Ron, pulling him into a friendly hug whilst Ron awkwardly patted his back and grunted his hello’s.

She turned to look at Malfoy. He wasn’t smiling, wasn’t talking, wasn’t engaging at all. His eyes were fixed on the table top, his jaw like iron.

‘Hello,’ Viktor said, leaning over her to offer his hand to Malfoy. ‘I think we met when I came to Hogwarts before.’ He motioned to Malfoy’s hair with a large hand. ‘I remember the white...Murphy, is it?’

Malfoy gritted his teeth so hard Hermione was surprised he didn’t crack one. ‘Malfoy.’ He muttered as he took Viktor’s hand in what looked to be a bone-crushing grip. She saw a brief flash of uncertainty light over Viktor’s features before he was smiling again.

‘You’re on daddy duty today, I see.’ He nodded down where Aurora was beginning to stir in her pram. ‘Your wife wasn’t able to join us?’

Malfoy flinched, his expression tightening even further. ‘No.’ He said, dropping Viktor’s hand. ‘My wife passed away six months ago, I’m afraid.’

Viktor’s face fell, mortification and shock slackening his features, his tan instantly replaced with a fierce pink blush. ‘Oh - I - I’m sorry.’ He stammered. ‘I wasn’t aware. I saw the - the -’ his brown eyes swept down to Malfoy’s ring and away again quickly. ‘I assumed -‘

Malfoy’s own eyes followed Viktor’s gaze, and he withdrew his hand quickly under the table, closing his fist and squeezing tightly.

‘My condolences.’ Viktor said again quietly. ‘I’m so sorry for your loss.’

Malfoy managed a small nod, but his eyes were icy, his pulse visibly pounding in his throat.

An awkward silence stretched out, the air thick with tension and Malfoy’s magic. Hermione could feel it coiling around him, writhing agitatedly, like a snake about to strike. She groped about under the table until she found his fingers, squeezing them gently in hers. After a second’s hesitation, she felt Malfoy squeezing back - the contact appearing to calm him slightly, his breathing coming a little easier.

Viktor’s face was still beetroot red. He rubbed an awkward hand over the back of his neck, looking anywhere but at Malfoy.

Eventually, Malfoy sat back, exhaling a long, slow breath. ‘I’ll go and get us a drink, love.’ He murmured, his voice low and quiet as he let go of her hand under the table.

‘Oh, thanks. I’ll have -‘ Hermione blurted, glad the silence was finally broken.

‘I know.’ His grey eyes flicked towards Viktor, then back to her. ‘I’ll get you a large glass. Are you okay to hold Aurora while I go to the bar?’

Hermione looked over to where Aurora was now wide awake in her pram, reaching up to grasp at the dragon toy that hung inside the hood and babbling to herself.

‘Of course. I’d love to.’ She held out her arms as Malfoy scooped the baby up, kissing the tip of her nose before passing her gently to Hermione.

Aurora accepted the transfer happily, gazing up at Hermione with huge grey eyes and swiping immediately at one of her dangling curls. Hermione laughed and shook her hair back over her shoulders, bouncing her a little. ‘Hello, darling.’ She said, smiling down at her. ‘Was that a lovely sleep?’

Aurora grinned back, a thin trail of dribble escaping down her chin.

Hermione looked up to see both Malfoy and Viktor watching her intently, their expressions almost a mirror image of the other, despite how opposite they were in appearance.

Malfoy cleared his throat, his eyes like flint as he placed a lilac muslin cloth and a full bottle down onto the table. ‘Just in case.’ He muttered, before turning and heading towards the bar.

Instantly, Theo was also on his feet, striding across the pub to stand with Malfoy as he ordered their drinks. They huddled together, their heads bent low in furtive, hushed conversation.

Opposite her, Neville had clearly watched the entire interaction, and now he glared at the back of Malfoy’s head, his mouth a hard, flat line. He jerked when Hannah hissed something into his ear, his gaze turning to his fiancé for the first time since they’d arrived. He nodded curtly at whatever Hannah had said, lifting his pint of lager and taking a long swallow.

Hannah’s own eyes flashed to Hermione, and she felt her skin prickle with discomfort and irritation, sweat breaking out on her upper lip.

She searched desperately for Harry down the other end of the table, wishing they’d sat closer together. He was half engaged in conversation with Seamus and Dean, but his gaze continuously flicked to where Theo stood at the bar.

As though he felt her staring at him, Harry turned and they each pulled a face. She was glad to see he shared her sentiments, his sympathetic smile making her feel a little less unmoored.

In her arms, Aurora began grumbling a little, fed up with being held lying down. Hermione stood up and tipped the baby onto her hip, sweeping her hair onto her opposite shoulder to avoid questing little hands. Aurora settled, happier now that she could see what was going on. She peered curiously past Hermione’s arm, taking in all the new sights of the pub restaurant.

‘She’s a lovely baby,’ Viktor said quietly, giving Aurora a hesitant smile which she didn’t return, her little brows furrowed as she studied the stranger’s face. ‘I feel terrible. I had no idea about her mother.’

‘It’s not your fault, Viktor.’ Hermione replied earnestly. ‘You weren’t to know.’

He nodded, not looking remotely convinced. ‘How do you two know each other?’ He asked. ‘You and Murphy. Were you friends at school?’

‘It’s Malfoy, and no. We weren’t friends at all - the opposite, actually.’ Hermione told him. ‘But I helped him with Aurora in the early days after his wife died, and we’ve become sort of friends since. Also, Draco’s best friend Theo is now going out with Harry, so we’re in each other's company a lot.’

‘Oh,’ Viktor said, sounding relieved. ‘He’s the friend of a friend, then.’

Hermione frowned, not sure that was what she’d actually said. She was about to correct him when a loud burst of scandalised laughter issued from the other end of the table.

‘Krum!’ Ginny called. ‘Come and listen to this bullshit. Dean thinks the Cannons can actually win the league this season. Sort him out, will you?’

Viktor turned to Hermione with an apologetic grin. ‘Just one moment, Herminny,’ he muttered. ‘I’ll be back.’ He stood and made his way down to where Dean, Seamus, Ginny and Ron were having a loud, animated conversation. Dean was already waving his hands around, vehemently defending his position. He fell silent when Viktor arrived, the entire group listening raptly to what the ‘Greatest Seeker In The World’ had to say on the matter.

‘Hermione,’ Luna called from where she sat next to Padma. She motioned with her hand. ‘Come and introduce Aurora to the other children.’

Hermione made her way round the table, taking the free seat next to Luna and propping Aurora up on her knees so that she could see Fred, who was being bounced on his own mother’s lap. Atlas, who was three, looked up from his colouring book momentarily, his long blonde curls falling into his eyes. He smiled shyly when Hermione said hello, but then went back to work, colouring a picture of a kneazle with a chunky blue crayon. Kiran and Mira were busy crawling around under the table, giggling secretively - clearly up to no good as usual.

Immediately, Fred and Aurora were entranced with each other. Fred leaned forward on his mother’s lap, his chubby fingers splayed as he reached towards Aurora, who watched him cautiously, her grey eyes wary. Her serious expression made her look so much like her father that Hermione couldn’t help but smile, her heart squeezing.

‘Who’s that, Freddie?’ Padma cooed. ‘It’s Auntie Mione and baby Aurora. Say hello to Aurora. Isn’t she sweet? Such a pretty baby.’

Across the bar, Malfoy had finished ordering the drinks. He turned and stopped, something undefinable in his expression as he took in the sight of Hermione, sitting with his daughter on her lap in amongst her friends.

Initially, she wondered if he might be annoyed, that he might not have wanted her to carry his baby around the pub, but when his eyes slid up to meet hers, Hermione’s breath caught in her throat, her heart suddenly fluttering in her chest. Malfoy looked almost…pleased, a dark glint of something like satisfaction in his eyes. He smiled, just a small pull at one side of his mouth, the first she’d seen since they’d left the Penthouse. Hermione smiled back, her stomach in knots. She found that she couldn’t look anywhere else.

From behind him, Theo said something to Malfoy, motioning to the drinks lined up on the bar. When he called him a second time, Malfoy blinked, finally cutting his eyes away from hers.

Hermione swallowed, forcing her attention back to Padma, who was still chattering to Aurora and Fred, laughing as Fred tried to hand Aurora the teddy he’d been clutching while Aurora stared uncertainly at him.

‘Theo’s nice, isn’t he?’ Padma said quietly, brown eyes raising to meet Hermione’s. ‘Friendly.’

‘He’s great,’ Hermione replied. ‘He and Harry are perfect for each other.’

‘It’s so weird to see Harry with a real boyfriend. I don’t think any of us thought we’d actually see the day. And a Slytherin too, that’s a turnout for the books.’

Hermione shrugged. ‘I guess those old rivalries don’t matter so much any more.’

‘Of course they don’t. We were children.’ Padma waved a dismissive hand. ‘It looks like Harry and Malfoy have managed to kiss and make up, too? The four of you looked very cosy on the way in.’

‘Yes, they have. Because Harry now knows what I do, Padma.’ Hermione said. ‘Malfoy isn’t the same person he was in school. He’s a good man who’s been through a lot. He’s just doing his best to move forward.’

‘I think it’s lovely,’ Luna interjected. ‘If Harry and Draco can put the past behind them, we all can.’

Padma nodded. ‘Oh, I agree. The past is in the past as far as I’m concerned, and I said as much to Ron. I made him promise to try with Malfoy too, not just Theo. I even suggested they might bond over dad things. Maybe meet up with the babies or something.’

Hermione tried to keep a straight face, but she almost snorted at the thought of Malfoy and Ron bonding. Tolerating each other seemed like the first logical step before moving on to anything as implausible as spending time alone together.

‘Maybe Draco could bring Aurora to your massage class?’ Luna suggested. ‘It’s good for babies to socialise. Does he take her to any baby groups already, Hermione, do you know?’

Hermione shook her head, smiling as she imagined Malfoy’s reaction to Padma’s massage class, picturing his face when the mums began chanting and singing, waving scarves around. ‘He’s not really the baby group type, I don’t think.’ She said tactfully. ‘But I’ll suggest it to him, of course.’

Aurora was now reaching out, aiming for one of Fred’s black curls. Hermione moved her gently back onto her shoulder, taking her out of grab radius and giving her little hand a kiss.

‘I can’t believe Viktor came to see you.’ Padma said, almost dreamily. ‘He’s so sweet, and so bloody gorgeous. Is he going to take you out again later? For some alone time, maybe?’ She wiggled her eyebrows suggestively and Hermione cringed.

‘I’m not sure how long he has free.’ She replied. ‘I doubt we’ll have time.’

Theo and Malfoy were now making their way back towards the table, drinks in hand. Hermione stood up, lifting Aurora back onto her hip. ‘I’d better get this one back to her dad.’

She bent down as she left, peering under the table. ‘Hello, you two.’ Mira squeaked and Kiran jumped, immediately dropping the wand he’d been trying to get out of his mother’s bag, clearly unaware of the child-lock charms all modern wands now came with as standard.

‘Auntie Mione!’ Hermione found herself momentarily mobbed as the two of them scrambled out from under the table and grabbed her around the knees, hugging her tightly.

‘Hi, my little squishes,’ Hermione murmured. ‘What were you up to under there?’

‘Whose baby is that?’ Kiran conveniently ignored the question, narrowing his eyes suspiciously at Aurora. ‘Did she come out of your tummy, Auntie Mione?’

‘No,’ she laughed softly. ‘She’s called Aurora. She belongs to my friend, Draco.’

Mira reached up to grasp Aurora’s toes through her little sock. ‘Does she want to play with us?’

‘She’s a bit small for that right now,’ Hermione replied. ‘But I’m sure she’d love to when she’s a bit older. I could come round to play soon, though? I’ll bring my healer’s bag so we can play hospitals again?’

The twins both jumped up and down with excitement at the suggestion, giving her knees another squeeze before disappearing back under the table.

Hermione made her way back to the table slowly, chatting to various people on the way. Malfoy sat back in his seat, watching her approach. He pushed her glass of wine towards her as she sat down, holding out his hands for Aurora, who had seen her dad and lunged towards him. He wrapped his arms around his baby and buried his face in her hair, as always, reminding Hermione of a man who’d gone far too long without the air he needed to breathe.

‘Did I just see you talking to boys, Aurora?’ He murmured softly against the top of her head. ‘Now what did daddy say about that?’

Hermione laughed. ‘I wouldn’t worry. She didn’t look too keen. Has she actually met any other babies before?’

Malfoy sighed and shook his head, looking embarrassed. ‘None.’ He said. ‘I haven’t… she only really knows us - our little group.’ She watched his cheeks colour. ‘I get…uncomfortable around too many people. But…I know I shouldn’t be putting that on Aurora. She needs to socialise.’

Sensing an imminent guilt spiral, Hermione immediately interjected. ‘She’s only little, Draco. There’s plenty of time. She might enjoy some baby groups soon, though?’

He frowned up at her, a line appearing between his eyebrows. ‘What do you mean? What’s a baby group?’

Hermione smiled, taking a sip of her wine. ‘I mean a group for babies and parents, so she can be around other children, experience different things. There are singing groups, walking groups…’ She paused for a moment and then decided it was worth a shot. ‘Padma runs a baby massage group. That might be nice? The massage could be something you do with Aurora in the evenings to calm her during the witching hour.’

Malfoy looked utterly confused now. ‘Baby massage? What in Merlin’s name-‘

Hermione laughed. ‘You lay the babies down on mats and then there are creams and oils you can use to rub over their skin and bodies using specific techniques - it’s relaxing for them, and for you as a parent. A good bonding experience.’

‘But - wouldn’t it be all…mums there?’ He asked. ‘I don’t know if I could… and the germs, all those people breathing down on her-‘ he was already shaking his head, aghast. ‘No. No way. I don’t think I’m the baby group type.’

She pursed her lips to hold back a smile, aware that he was repeating her own words back to her, even as she kept trying. ‘It would be good to meet with some other parents, Draco. You can share your experiences, get advice from people who’ve already been where you are.’

Malfoy was chewing his lip, his expression conflicted. His eyes flicked down to Aurora again, and Hermione could see how badly he wanted to do what was best for her, even if it was incredibly hard for him.

‘Why don’t you think about it?’ She said eventually. ‘Maybe I could come with you for the first time? I can just play with Kiran and Mira and stay out of the way.’

He looked up at Padma, who noticed and smiled back across the table. Hermione watched him battle with himself, a muscle twitching in his cheek. He was quiet for several seconds before he turned his eyes back to her and nodded. ‘Okay. I’ll give it a go. If you’re going to be there.’

Hermione’s stomach swooped as she was reminded suddenly of the first days at the hospital, his first insistence of ‘only you, Granger.’ It still felt as disorienting as it had the first time.

‘Of course.’ She promised. ‘I think Aurora will love it. Maybe you’d enjoy it too. It’s good to meet new people, maybe even make some new friends.’

He looked dubious. ‘I don’t need any more new friends, thanks.’ His eyes sparkled mischievously as he added. ‘I can’t seem to get rid of the last one I made. This curly-haired girl. She just won’t leave me alone.’

‘Oi!’ She laughed, punching him lightly on the arm. ‘I think you’ll find it’s me that can’t get rid of you.’

Malfoy grinned so widely that the eye crinkles made an appearance, waging war on Hermione’s emotional stability. ‘Oh really?’ He leaned closer, his voice low and confidential. ‘You haven’t been trying very hard now, have you, Granger?’

They were still smirking at each other when Hermione heard Viktor cough discreetly behind her. She turned and blushed, unsure how long he’d been sitting there.

‘Oh! Sorry,’ she stuttered, turning her body away from Malfoy, who was still smirking.

Viktor said nothing, a small frown forming a divot between his heavy brows. He picked up his menu and began scanning it, running his finger down the options. ‘I think I’ll have the lamb.What about you, Herminny?’

‘She doesn’t like lamb.’ Malfoy muttered under his breath, almost too low for Viktor to hear.

‘Uh, I don’t like lamb,’ she echoed, elbowing Malfoy surreptitiously at her side. ‘I think I’ll have the vegetable tart.’

Viktor read the menu again. ‘Oh, that sounds nice. Yes, I’ll have the same.’ She ignored further muttering from her left, taking a large gulp of her Pinot Grigio.

In Malfoy’s arms, Aurora began to whine quietly, arching her back and twisting. He immediately leaned over and swiped the bottle from the table, guiding it to her lips. Aurora latched on, quieting again, although she continued fidgeting distractedly.

For the next ten minutes, Hermione made small talk with Viktor as the table gave their orders to the waiter and Malfoy fed Aurora. Inevitably, the focus soon turned to Theo, who was warm and engaging, laughing when Seamus ribbed him about his job at the bank, smiling smugly when Harry jumped in to defend him, leaning into Harry’s chest. He looked utterly at ease, asking questions and joking around as though he’d been part of the group forever.

Hermione thought she was actually witnessing Harry Potter fall in love. His eyes shone as he watched Theo speak, his lips curved up in a proud, proprietorial smile, his expression hungry. She wasn’t in the least bit surprised when his hand crept up to cup Theo’s jaw and turn his face towards his mouth, a possessive glint in his eyes. Harry kissed Theo once before breaking away to whisper something in his ear. Theo shuddered and nodded dazedly, his eyes glazing over.

After a few deep breaths, Harry pushed back in his chair and rose from the table. ‘Just popping to the loo,’ he announced, throwing a wholly unnecessary look in Theo’s direction.

Theo remained seated for at least another full minute, chatting to Dean and Luna absently, his eyes on the bar door. At her side, Hermione heard Malfoy whisper, ‘Three… two… one…’

‘Ahem,’ Theo stood up. ‘I’m just going to… wash my hands.’

She giggled as Malfoy shook his head. ‘About as subtle as a Bombarda, those two.’



* * *



Harry and Theo still weren’t back by the time the meals began arriving at the table. A poor, unsuspecting Ron had attempted to go and retrieve them and come stumbling back immediately, his face pale. ‘It’s okay,’ he muttered as Padma put a comforting hand on his knee. ‘I didn’t need my eyes anyway.’

Seamus, Dean and Ginny, who had immediately clocked what was happening, burst out laughing, as did Hermione and Padma. Luna, who was busy playing with Atlas, was the only adult who seemed unaware, other than Neville and Hannah, who were now talking in hushed, snappy voices opposite Malfoy. Hermione watched Neville down his beer, rolling his eyes at something Hannah had said.

Ten minutes later, with their food now completely cold, both men returned to the table, their faces pink, lips puffy. ‘Really?’ Ron demanded. ‘At a restaurant? In the middle of the day?’

‘You may as well get used to that.’ Malfoy drawled from her side. ‘It’s like living with a pair of graphorns in mating season half the time.’

Ron hesitated for a moment, as though he wasn’t sure Malfoy was actually speaking to him, then he laughed. ‘Yeah,’ he said awkwardly. ‘Yeah, that’s a pretty accurate description, I’d say. Good one, Malfoy.’

The rest of the meal passed quickly with the distraction of the food. They talked as a group as they ate, Viktor filling them in on his training regimen, giving them the inside scoop on the various teams. He’d managed to get the World Cup tickets Ron had asked for, and Ron was over the moon, scrambling around the table to shake Viktor’s hand gratefully, looking slightly like he was about to cry.

Hannah had left the table halfway through the main course and hadn’t returned. Neville sat silently opposite them, draining what Hermione estimated to be his fifth pint. He’d shaken his head when Harry had tried to ask what was wrong, insisting he was fine. But he was uncharacteristically quiet, even for Neville, a hard look on his face that Hermione didn’t think she’d ever seen before.

Malfoy had put Aurora into a high chair and was attempting to spoon-feed her bits of blended broccoli between rushed mouthfuls of his own lunch. Trying solids was a new development for Aurora, and unfortunately, more of it had ended up in her hair and over her face than in her mouth.

Hermione helped as best she could, fetching the wipes from the bag and holding Aurora’s hands out of the way as Malfoy attempted to wipe her down, both of them laughing when Aurora blew a raspberry, spitting green blobs all over Malfoy’s T-shirt.

‘Okay, I get the hint, sweetheart,’ he grinned, switching the broccoli pot for one with what looked like strawberries instead. ‘Daddy doesn’t like broccoli either.’

Hermione was still helping to clean broccoli out of Malfoy’s fringe when she suddenly heard Viktor’s chair scrape back behind her. She turned to find him standing, pulling his coat off the back of his chair.

‘Viktor? You’re leaving already?’

Viktor didn’t reply for a moment, his brown eyes trying and failing to meet hers. ‘Yes, Herminny.’ He said eventually. ‘I have to get back to France. We have a team briefing tonight.’

‘Oh,’ a horrible, guilty feeling began creeping over her. ‘I’m sorry. I didn’t realise you needed to leave so soon.’

Viktor said nothing, pushing a hand in his pocket and withdrawing a thick wad of galleons, which he placed on the table.

‘Aww, you’re going already, Krum?’ Ginny called from the other end of the table. ‘I thought you said you had two days off?’

Hermione turned to look at him as the rest of the table murmured their dissent. ‘You can’t go yet,’ Seamus groaned. ‘You were halfway through telling us about that potion-doping scandal.’

‘Sorry,’ Viktor mumbled, still not looking at Hermione. ‘Something came up. I’ll see you all soon. Thank you, Ginevra, for the invite. See you back in France.’ He raised one hand in farewell and then turned, walking out of the pub without another word.

The table fell silent as Ginny frowned over at Hermione. ‘What happened? Why is he leaving?’

‘I - I don’t know,’ Hermione pushed back her own chair. ‘I’ll go and see him off.’ She didn’t look at Malfoy as she turned to follow Viktor, but she felt his eyes on her, along with the rest of the table. Neville was the only person she knew definitely wasn’t watching. He was still too busy eyeballing Malfoy.



* * *



Viktor was already at the exit by the time she caught him. He stopped when she called his name, turning around, his face set, his expression closed.

‘Viktor,’ she breathed. ‘I’m sorry. I think I might have-‘ he held a hand up to stop her, his eyes already resigned.

‘Herminny, please.’ He said. ‘It’s okay, really.’

‘What do you mean?’ Hermione began. ‘I’m not sure what happened. I barely got the chance to speak to you. I’m sorry, I didn’t realise you were coming-‘

‘Stop.’ Viktor repeated, his voice soft, his expression pained. ‘You don’t need to explain anything to me. In fact, I really don’t want to hear it.’

Hermione paused. ‘Viktor, I-‘

He shook his head. ‘No, Herminny. There were plenty of chances for you to talk to me today if you wanted to. Just like there have been plenty of chances for you to visit me in France when I offered, or for me to visit you, or even for us to just speak on the phone. You haven’t taken any of those chances. I understood. I know you said you were busy with work. But…’ he closed his eyes for a moment, swallowing. ‘There’s a saying I’ve heard. I’m not sure if I have it right…it says if you wanted to, you would.’ Hermione bit her lip, inexplicably close to tears.

‘I tried my best.’ Viktor said. ‘I told you how I feel about you, what I wanted for us. Initially, I thought maybe I’d just come on too strong, that you were overwhelmed by the Prophet article. But after today, I know that’s not the case. You didn’t look pleased to see me, and you’ve barely spoken to me. I feel shit, Herminny.’ Viktor looked up at her, and his brown eyes were so hurt that Hermione felt sick. Utterly ashamed at how she’d treated him, knowing he was right.

‘If you wanted to, you would.’ He said softly. ‘But you don’t.’

Hermione couldn’t speak. Even now, Viktor was being nice to her. He wasn’t shouting or even telling her outright that she’d embarrassed him, which she was certain she had done.

She must be mad, Hermione thought. Was she really about to effectively put an end to any further chance of a relationship with Viktor? A man who had continually been kind and respectful towards her. A man with no complications, who wanted to give her the world, now. Viktor was ready to provide her with everything she’d ever wanted and more. So why was she holding out? What was it she possibly wanted that Viktor couldn’t offer her?

An image flashed up in her mind - sharp grey eyes, a wicked smile, a silver ring glinting on a long-fingered hand. Hermione exhaled a long, shaking breath.

‘I’m so sorry, Viktor,’ she said quietly, her throat thick, shame burning its way up her oesophagus. ‘I never intended to lead you on. You’re a wonderful man, truly. Any girl would be lucky to have you.’

‘Except you.’ Viktor said quietly. ‘You don’t want me…do you?’

Hermione’s chest was heavy as she forced herself to look at him, forced herself to be honest. ‘No,’ she whispered. ‘I’m sorry, Viktor…I …I don’t.’

He smiled ruefully. ‘Thank you for telling me the truth,’ he said. ‘I wish you the best, Herminny. I know one day, you will make someone very happy.’

He leaned in and kissed her cheek. ‘Take care, Lyubima.’ And with that, Viktor Apparated away.



* * *



When she had finally gathered herself, Hermione made her way back into the pub.

‘Everything alright, Mi?’ Harry asked. ‘Has Viktor left?’

‘Yes,’ Hermione nodded, hoping she didn’t look as upset as she felt. ‘He had a team meeting called and had to leave unexpectedly.’ Harry nodded, everyone except for Ginny seeming to accept her response.

‘What went wrong?’ Ginny asked as she walked past. ‘He was so excited to see you. It’s almost like you don’t want to have sex with anyone, Mi.’

Hermione paused, looking up to find Malfoy watching her, Aurora now fast asleep, curled up in his arms. ‘Not now, Gin.’ She muttered. ‘Not here. I’ll call you tomorrow and we can talk about it properly.’

In her absence, the table had cleared a little. Ron, Harry and Theo were at the bar, Fred also fast asleep over Ron’s shoulder. Seamus and Dean were sitting next to Malfoy, making a valiant attempt to engage him in conversation. Padma and Luna had apparently taken the children out to the beer garden for a run around. Neither Neville nor Hannah were anywhere to be seen.

Hermione needed to splash some cold water on her face. She made her way to the bathroom, pausing when she met Neville on his way back from the men’s. He was stumbling a little, his eyes bloodshot. He pulled up when he saw her, sighing loudly and pushing his hands into his pockets. Hermione continued walking towards him, waiting to see if he was actually going to just ignore her. It appeared that was exactly what Neville intended to do.

‘Seriously?’ She huffed as he stepped past. ‘That’s how it is now, Neville? You can’t even bring yourself to speak to me all of a sudden?’

‘Leave it, Mione.’ Neville bit out. ‘I’m not in the mood.’

‘Oh, you’re not? Because it kind of seems as though you are.’ Hermione retorted, already emotional from her confrontation with Viktor. ‘You’ve spent the whole meal trying to bait Malfoy into a fight, staring at him across the table. You’ve made lunch uncomfortable for everyone.’

Neville clenched his jaw, staring down at the ground at her feet. ‘It’s just hard to watch, that’s all, Mi.’

Hermione sighed. ‘Neville, look. I know it must be strange to suddenly have Malfoy around. He was awful to you when we were younger. I understand. He was awful to me, too. But we’re adults now. None of us are the same people we were. Malfoy has been through a lot, too. He didn’t get away without suffering.’

‘It’s not even that,’ Neville said heatedly. ‘I mean, I still think he’s an absolute wanker no matter what you and Harry say. But that’s not what pisses me off. It’s you. For the first time today, I saw you. I saw what you really look like, Hermione, and it hurts.’ Neville’s voice cracked on the last word.

‘What's that supposed to mean?' Hermione held her hands out, confused. ‘I look the same as I always have.’

Neville shook his head. ‘No. You don’t. Today I finally saw what you look like when you're all in. When you’re fully committed - not constantly feeling your way around the walls for an exit.’ His blue eyes were resigned despite the bitterness in his tone. 'After everything that's happened, everything he's done - it's him. Draco fucking Malfoy is the one who gets to have all of you.’

Hermione felt a fresh rush of frustration surge through her. ‘What are you talking about, Neville?’ She burst out. ‘Malfoy doesn’t have me. We’re friends, that’s all.’

Neville scoffed. ‘Yeah, right. Keep telling yourself that, Mione. Why do you think Krum left? He was embarrassed because he didn’t get a look in. You spent the whole meal flirting with Malfoy, holding his kid, acting like she’s your baby when she’s not. If his wife were alive, do you really think Malfoy would be sitting there with you? He’s using you, Hermione. I thought you, of all people, would be smart enough to realise that.’

‘Oh fuck you, Neville!’ Hermione snapped, his words hitting her straight in her gut. ‘And where’s your fiancé? I don’t know if you noticed, but she’s not here either.’ Instantly, Hermione regretted her words, angry with herself for stooping to Neville’s level of pettiness.

Neville’s face had turned pale, his eyes cold. ‘Don’t talk about Hannah.’ He muttered. ‘It’s not that simple. We have… things going on.’

Hermione was about to reply when the door to the bar suddenly pushed open and Malfoy stepped through, ducking his head to avoid the frame. He was holding Aurora, a fresh nappy and a set of wipes in his hand. He stopped short when he saw Hermione and Neville, immediately sensing the atmosphere in the hallway. Hermione swiped angrily at the tears she hadn’t even noticed she was crying, furious that she’d once again let Neville affect her so deeply.

‘Oh,’ Malfoy muttered. ‘Sorry, I was just…’ he trailed off, catching sight of Hermione’s face. ‘Granger?’ His eyebrows drew together, concern darkening his features. ‘Are you okay?’

Neville snorted. ‘There you go, Mi. Your knight in shining armour has arrived.’ He blew out a frustrated breath. ‘I need to leave.’ Neville began to stride towards the exit, almost shoulder barging Malfoy on the way past.

Malfoy stepped deftly sideways as his hand suddenly shot out, blocking Neville’s path. His stare was murderous, his body unnervingly still as he turned to Neville with an expression Hermione hadn’t seen since the funeral home.

‘Are you insane, Longbottom?’ He hissed. ‘If you’d just knocked into my daughter, you’d be on your fucking back.’

‘I’m sure I would,’ Neville snapped, his words a little slurred. ‘You’d fucking love that, wouldn’t you, Malfoy. My childhood bully, back to throw his weight around.’

Neville went to walk away again, pushing open the bar door aggressively. Malfoy growled, and without Hermione actually even seeing Malfoy move, Neville was suddenly yanked backwards and slammed against the wall, the door swinging closed with a bang.

Malfoy’s nostrils flared as he handed Aurora carefully to Hermione, his eyes glittering with fury. His voice was low and dangerous when he turned back to Neville, stepping into his space. Neville held his eye, his expression satisfied. He’d goaded Malfoy, and he’d gotten a reaction.

‘Don’t think I’m fucking blind, Longbottom.’ Malfoy murmured. ‘That I didn’t notice the way you’ve been looking at me during this lunch. I’ve actually let it slide, given you the grace you fucking deserve after how I treated you as a boy. I was a prick at school, an absolute twat. I accept that and I apologise unreservedly to you for the things I said and did. You can hate me if you like, I really don’t give a fuck. But I’ve just walked out here and Granger -‘ he pointed a finger at Hermione. ‘Is crying. And that -‘ the finger moved to jab Neville in the sternum. ‘That I won’t let slide.’

Neville’s chest was heaving. His blue eyes equally as tempestuous as Malfoy’s. They darted down to Malfoy’s pointing finger and suddenly, he froze, his breath catching in his throat. ‘What the fuck is that?’

Malfoy also froze, following Neville’s eyeline.

‘What…why is Hermione’s scar on your arm?’

‘Neville,’ Hermione began.

‘No! What the fuck?’ Despite Malfoy’s finger digging into his chest, Neville took a step forward - closer to Malfoy. He was a big man, as tall as Malfoy and broader, heavier set, where Malfoy was lean. Malfoy shoved him back against the wall, but Neville barely seemed to notice, even when his back connected hard with the stone. He turned to Hermione, his eyes beseeching. ‘Mione, why does Draco Malfoy have the word he watched his own aunt carve into you, on his arm?’

‘Because I’m not the boy who watched any more.’ Malfoy said quietly, pulling Neville’s attention back to him. ‘I took it for that very reason. I’m doing everything I can to prove to her -‘ he pointed at Hermione again. ‘That I’m a better man. That I’m in any way worthy of being part of her life.’

Neville looked between Hermione and Malfoy for a long moment, his mind seeming to take in what Malfoy had done as they slid down again, back to the faded letters on his forearm. He sagged, all the fight draining out of him at once. ‘You - you took her scar?’ Neville’s voice was hoarse. ‘You took the thing she hated the most about herself, and now you just...what? Walk around with the word Mudblood on your body instead?’

Malfoy nodded. ‘Proudly.’

Neville fell silent. He attempted to push Malfoy’s hand off his chest and, sensing the change in Neville’s demeanour, Malfoy allowed it, stepping back to give him the space.

‘You can’t keep doing this, Nev.’ Hermione said softly. ‘You made your choice, you’re engaged to Hannah. You’re making it impossible for us to stay friends, and you’re ruining your relationship in the process. Hannah loves you and you’re hurting her.’

Neville nodded, his eyes unbearably sad when he looked at her. ‘I know.’ He admitted. He seemed to have forgotten Malfoy was there, his eyes on her face as he continued. ‘For a long time, I thought I was over you. I tried to move on. I told myself that it was just you. That you were committed to your job, that you didn’t have the capacity to really let yourself love someone after what we went through in the war. But now I see that you do have the capacity for it. Just not for me.’ Hermione remained silent, her words stuck in her chest as she found herself needing to be brutally honest for the second time that day. Regardless, her silence told Neville all he needed to know. He shook his head, taking another step back. ‘I need to go and find Hannah. I - I can’t keep doing this to her. It’s not fair.’

He finally looked back at Malfoy, seeming to be about to say something, then his eyes flicked to Aurora, and he closed it again. ‘I’m going to go.’ He said. ‘Sorry, Mione.’

Neville began walking towards the door before turning, blue eyes burning into Malfoy’s, his jaw clenched. ‘I’m very sorry for your loss.’ He muttered, and then Neville was gone too, pushing through the bar door, leaving Malfoy and Hermione alone in the aftermath.



* * *



Hermione hadn’t stayed long after that. She’d been unable to say anything much to Malfoy, distraught, guilty and embarrassed by the events of the day.

They’d walked back out into the pub, finding most people still oblivious, involved in their own conversations or distracted by the children. Harry and Theo were sitting at the table, utterly wrapped up in each other, blissfully ignorant of the emotional turmoil Hermione had been through with not one, but two men over the course of just a three-hour lunch.

She fought the urge to message him herself, craving some reassurance that she wasn’t imagining whatever was going on between them. She desperately wanted to know what he’d been going to say before Harry had interrupted. Did he regret what had happened? Maybe he was worried Hermione had gotten the wrong idea, because essentially, nothing had changed. Malfoy was still wearing his ring, he’d made no attempt to call her, or to see her. Maybe it had just been a brief moment of madness. He was probably just lonely, after all.



* * *



Tuesday dragged out painfully. The ward was relatively quiet, and so there was time for the staff to talk. Inevitably, the main topic of conversation was the grant funding and the next day’s presentations. Hermione’s nerves were in pieces, especially when Ernie sought her out to wish her luck, his own confidence worryingly apparent. She spent her shift on edge, snapping at Katie unnecessarily over a briefly misplaced file, bluntly cutting down a father who’d seemed more concerned with where the canteen was than his labouring wife. She’d chosen to work through her lunch break, spending it sitting by the side of a mother who was struggling with pain after a particularly difficult emergency delivery. By the time she was due to leave the hospital, Hermione was irritable and flustered, the nerves she’d tried hard to ignore for weeks threatening to take over.

Without Harry, the flat felt cold and empty when she got home. Hermione found herself standing listlessly at the fridge, her nervous stomach rejecting all available dinner options. Eventually, she sat down with a mug of herbal tea, spreading out her parchment notes in front of her, checking and double-checking her numbers and rehearsing her lines until she thought she might go mad, the letters all blurring into one. Finally, when she couldn’t look at it any more, she headed to bed, sliding between the covers with a sigh.



* * *



Hermione fought the urge to look at her phone for at least twenty minutes, but eventually the temptation became too much. She sighed, picking it up from the nightstand and opening Malfoy’s photo again. She bit her lip as her eyes roved over the image she’d now mostly memorised, gliding down the lines of his body, over each pale scar, the dips of muscle, the delicate lines of his vine tattoos. She couldn’t stop her eyes being pulled lower, to the outline beneath his shorts, her breath hitching, her body growing warm beneath the bedcovers. She had done that to him. Malfoy sent her that picture, surely knowing she’d see what she’d done to him, how his body responded to the sight of hers.

Hermione closed her eyes, allowing her fingertips to trail lightly over her collarbone, sliding them down over the satin that covered her breasts, her nipples tightening as she imagined her touch was his. Her back arched, her mind helpfully filling in what Draco might look like leaning over her, silver eyes fixed on her face.

When her phone rang, Hermione was so startled that she dropped it onto the floor. She picked it back up with trembling fingers, staring at the flashing display.

Malfoy was calling her.

Hermione glanced at the time in the corner of the screen. Why was Malfoy calling at two am?

She pressed the green phone icon, hoping he couldn’t hear the slight shake in her voice as it connected.

‘Draco?’

There was silence on the other end of the line. She waited, her heart in her mouth.

She knew he was there. She could hear his quiet breaths, the crackle of static coming over the line.

‘Is everything okay?’ Hermione asked, unnerved by the protracted silence.

‘Granger.’

Her name was a whisper, more of an exhale than solid inflection. But even so, Hermione could hear how much those two ragged syllables cost him. Malfoy sounded like he was in pain, his voice gravelly, rough with emotion. She let her eyes fall shut.

‘What are you doing to me, love?’ He sighed, and Hermione shuddered, pressing the phone tightly to her ear. She fought to keep her breathing quiet as Malfoy continued. ‘I shouldn’t have sent you that photo, Granger. I shouldn’t be calling you now.’

Her throat constricted, her stomach dropping despite the fact that she’d been fully expecting him to say it.

Malfoy’s regret had already taken hold.

He let out another long breath down the phone.

‘The problem is…I can’t seem to make myself stop.’

Hermione was ruined. She was utterly ruined by Draco Malfoy. She’d never want anyone else, never want a man this badly ever again. She knew it in that very moment, her entire body thrumming with anticipation as she waited for him to continue.

‘I don’t know what to do,’ he whispered hoarsely.

‘The guilt, it's - it’s breaking me.’

‘Draco,’ she finally managed to make her voice work. ‘I understand. It’s only been six months. You’re grieving, still adjusting.’

‘I’m just so sad for Astoria.’ He murmured, sounding worryingly close to tears. ‘I’m sad that she died before getting to be a mother, that she lived such a shit, lonely life with a man she didn’t choose. I didn’t - I didn’t love her the way I should have, I realise that now. She died without ever really knowing happiness. She died without ever feeling this.’

‘Feeling what, Draco?’ Hermione whispered. ‘What is it that you feel?’

The silence drew on again.

‘I-‘ he paused. ‘Granger, I think I - ‘

The silence at the other end of the line was suddenly broken by a loud, high-pitched wail. Aurora’s cries gradually grew louder, muffled by the phone but still equally as affecting.

‘Shit,’ Malfoy muttered. ‘I need to go to her.’

‘Y-yes.’ Hermione stuttered, her heart sinking as she realised the conversation was over. ‘Of course.’

‘Good luck for tomorrow.’ He said. ‘We’ll be there, alright? As soon as your presentation has finished, Aurora and I will be at the hospital waiting for you…’ he hesitated. ‘As long as that’s what you want?’

‘Yes,’ Hermione breathed. ‘Of course…Draco, that’s what I want.’

He sounded relieved. ‘Then we’ll be there.’

‘Alright…I’ll see you both tomorrow.’

‘You’ll be amazing. I know it.’

Hermione wasn’t ready for the call to end. She wanted him to tell her how he felt, wanted to just listen to the sound of his voice. She didn’t want to be alone in the flat, where all her doubts could find her. But Aurora was Malfoy’s priority, and she always would be. It was part of what made him so painfully wonderful.

‘Goodnight, Draco.’ She whispered.

‘Night, Granger…sleep well.’

Hermione hung up the phone and immediately burst into tears.



Chapter End Notes

Dracooooooo 😭😭 This man is so deep in his survivors guilt, I think we can definitely see he’s not thinking clearly. He’s trying so hard to fight something he thinks is ‘wrong,’ out of respect for Astoria but there’s so much he still needs to tell Hermione that he just can’t quite bring himself to say! I feel it would be OOC for this Draco for him to just lay it all on the line over the phone, but I promise he will. Please know that these feelings he’s having for Hermione are all so new for him. He’s trying his best to do the right thing but he’s just SO DOWN BAD!

Chapter 21: I won’t cry, I won’t cry


Chapter Notes

Hello!!! Firstly, I need to apologise that this is a little late. Partially I can blame the cloudflare issue, but I need to be honest too. The chapter I’m posting now is probably version number 17474738 (at least). I’ve agonised over it, researched it, deleted it and edited it a million times and this is the version I’ve settled on.

Unfortunately, although I know it’s just fanfic, I sometimes get extremely affected by imposter syndrome or crisis of confidence, and this fic occasionally brings it out of me! The main reason is because I care - I really want to write the best story I can and sometimes it feels really difficult.

As always, I hope I managed to get the tone right. Initially I planned for more time to have passed before we get to this stage, but D and H’s relationship has developed so naturally and l think it’s important to note that Draco’s marriage is a different situation than most of us can relate to IRL and so I feel like however he responds is personal to this particular version of him.

There are medical terms used in this chapter which may not be 100% accurate and if so I apologise! I’m not in the medical field- be gentle with me! Haha

I’m sorry I haven’t gotten to the comments yet. I absolutely love them and have read every single one! I will respond now I’ve stopped having my meltdown.

Thank you to Blackphoenix_22 (The Dramione Effect podcast), Smudgesonapage and Didsomeonesay_dracomalfoy for their encouragement and patience this week! I know it hasn’t been easy!

LASTLY! (Sorry!) I found out a few weeks ago that I’ve been nominated for this years advent and I am so honoured and excited! Look out for a new (emotional) one shot from me coming some time in December!

The support and love on this fic continues to blow me away. It feels like a little SBM family and I love you all! Thank you for reading!

Nicky!



See the end of the chapter for more notes



Hermione watched Wednesday morning arrive, the sky gradually changing from blue to pink to orange as the day dawned. She lay on her side and blinked into the watery light, letting the rays warm her skin, soothing the exhausted ache in her bones.

Once again, she’d spent the night lying awake, replaying the things Malfoy had said on the phone, imagining all the things he might have said if he hadn’t been interrupted. A small nagging voice insisted that she was being selfish, that Malfoy would be better off without her in his life, torturing him with guilt.

But there had been something else in his voice, layered just beneath the pain on the surface - a need that mirrored her own so intensely she was powerless against it. She wanted to hear it again.

Just this once, Hermione ached to be able to actually take what she really wanted, to not always be tied by an intractable sense of morality or a deafening voice of reason. For the first time in her life, she wanted to be selfish.

At six am, her phone beeped with a message. It was a photo of Malfoy and Aurora, lying in his bed, Aurora in the foreground, Draco at the back - two smiling, silver-eyed peas in a pod. There was a message to accompany the photo.

Thinking of you. Sending you good luck wishes from us. See you later on X

Hermione ran her finger over their beautiful faces, over the identically curving lips, the tumble of sleep-ruffled blonde hair.

There were so many things she wanted to say to Malfoy in reply, but now wasn’t the time. She had something else important to do first. Sighing, she settled for a single letter, hoping that somehow, it might convey even a small part of how she felt.

X

A few moments later, it beeped again

X

Hermione smiled, hugging the phone to her chest briefly, before forcing herself to get out of bed.

It beeped again several times whilst she was getting ready, kind messages of support coming through from Padma and Ron, Luna and Ginny. A photo message popped up from Harry, and Hermione opened it eagerly, her spirits soaring when she found a photo of him and Theo outside the Duomo Di Milano, seemingly taken the night before. Theo’s lips were pressed to Harry’s neck, only a flash of blue visible as he smirked sideways at the camera. Harry grinned straight into the lens, his face alight with joy.

You’re going to be amazing today. He’d written. Love you so much X

Throughout the course of their adult life, Harry had always been the person Hermione turned to when she needed emotional support. He was the one constant in her world, there whenever she needed a cuddle, a cry, to shout or hit things. To be without him now was rough, but looking at the photograph, witnessing Harry’s long-awaited happiness so clearly - it was exactly the serotonin boost she needed.

The night before, Hermione had selected an outfit she thought looked professional yet assured - a black pencil-cut shift dress, a set of patent Prada heels and several pieces of simple silver jewellery. She slipped the dress on, examining herself in the mirror and turning, satisfied it was the look she was going for. After a moment of indecision, she settled on gathering half of her hair off her face with a claw clip, leaving the rest of her curls to tumble to her elbows.

In the quiet kitchenette, she gulped a scalding hot coffee as she gathered her notes, pushing them all into a leather briefcase and sealing it shut. There was nothing more she could do, no other way she could prepare. She was as ready as she was ever going to be.

With a final deep breath, Hermione twisted on the spot and Apparated straight into the hospital foyer.



* * *



‘Hermione? We’re ready for you‘.

Biting her lip, Hermione turned to see Chief Healer Jennings beckoning her towards the Auditorium doors. Ernie had left several minutes before, a cocky grin on his face and a swagger in his step. ‘Good luck in there,’ he’d smirked. ‘It’s bloody brutal.’

She’d scowled at his back as he sauntered towards the atrium, wishing less people were around so she could wandlessly trip him flat on his face. Hermione wasn’t stupid, she knew an intimidation tactic when she saw it.

But now, as Healer Jennings waited, eyebrows raised expectantly, Hermione felt her nerves settle in the pit of her stomach, a quiet, insistent voice in her head telling her once again that she should have chosen something simpler, less controversial. She pushed the thoughts away, gritting her teeth, as she rose from her chair.

Healer Jennings smiled. ‘Don’t be nervous, Hermione. You can do this with your eyes closed.’

She nodded, although now that the moment had come, any confidence she did have seemed to have evaporated into thin air. Heels clicking, she followed Healer Jennings into the small Auditorium, her stomach swooping as she got her first glimpse of the audience. As expected, most of the seats were full - a mix of healers, mediwix and other peers all waiting to see what this year’s candidates had to offer for the grant application. She caught sight of Katie and Danielle in amongst the small crowd, grateful for their presence and encouraging smiles. Healer Jennings led her towards the long table where the six witches and wizards that made up the hospital’s medical board were waiting. Formal introductions were conducted, and then a bald, round-faced man she knew to be Crispin Biggs, Chair of the board, cleared his throat.

‘Welcome, Miss Granger, and congratulations on reaching this stage in the application process. As you know, this year’s competition was particularly fierce.’

Hermione nodded, nerves preventing anything more.

‘You now have twenty minutes in which to present your proposal for the St Mungo’s Medical Advancement Grant, at the end of which we will allow a further ten minutes for questioning.’ He motioned with a heavily ringed hand. ‘When you’re ready, you may begin. Good luck.’

Palms sweating, Hermione moved to the stage and turned to face the audience, blinking into the spotlight. At the back of the room, she vaguely registered the door to the Auditorium opening and closing again as she shuffled her notes, laying them out on the lectern.

With a final bolstering inhale, she flicked her wand, bringing up a projected image of the first page of her presentation.

Signature Transfusion:

A New Future For Magical Obstetrics

By

Healer Hermione Jean Granger

As she turned back to the audience, movement from the back of the room caught her attention and she froze, breath catching in her throat.

Harry, Theo and Malfoy were moving through the rows of chairs, making their way towards the very centre of the auditorium. They settled into the last free seats, Harry and Theo waving enthusiastically, Malfoy simply holding her gaze, his lips tipped up into a small, reassuring smile. Aurora was sitting on his lap, peering around the Auditorium, fascinated by the lights overhead.

Hermione’s already pounding heart stuttered in her chest, the room falling away as she smiled back, unable to believe that they were actually there. Instantly, she was so much less alone, despite the fact that she was the only person on the stage.

With a new resolve, she let out the breath she’d been holding, aimed a Sonorous at her neck and began to speak.



* * *



Twenty minutes later, there was silence in the Auditorium as Hermione reached the final section of her presentation. ‘Thank you for listening.’ She concluded. ‘I am now open for questions.’

Immediately, a low murmur rippled over the crowd, several hands shooting into the air. From the front of the room, Barney Trott (Minister for Health) spoke first.

‘Miss Granger. You realise that what you have proposed here is completely unheard of in magical maternity care? In fact, in magical medicine, full stop?’

‘I do, Minister.’ Hermione replied simply. ‘But what is progression without change? Here at St Mungo’s, we are fortunate to have the advantage of magic to aid in birth and recovery, however, whilst we continue to use traditional healing for managing complications, we are ignoring simple Muggle methods that can help patients beyond the limits of magic.’ When the minister didn’t respond, Hermione continued. ‘The charm I have developed can cross-match magical signatures to identify compatible donor blood. This can be used to treat our most critical patients when replenishment is not a viable option.’

‘And can you give an example of when this might be, Healer Granger?’

Hermione nodded. ‘Muggle physicians often use transfusion as a way to treat disorders or deficiencies in the blood. In the magical world, when a person needs blood, we use replenishers. But if that blood is low on the things the patient needs, or is harbouring something potentially dangerous, we are simply regenerating harmful blood cells. Transfusion would allow us to replace those cells with healthy ones. From an obstetric point of view, this can be used during pregnancy to reduce the risk of premature delivery, support babies in the neonatal unit, and -‘ she couldn’t look at Malfoy- ‘during and after events such as a traumatic birth.’

‘Surely there are several ethical considerations to be made here?’ This was Hilary Ladock, renowned magical scientist and the author of one of Hermione’s favourite books. ‘As well as the matter of bloodlines? Some people may refuse this treatment on principle.’

Hermione knew exactly the type of people Healer Ladock was talking about, and she had been fully expecting the question.

‘Unfortunately, Professor Ladock, I believe it’s attitudes like this that have held the magical world back for far too long. Of course, every patient has a choice in the treatment they accept, but in the twenty-first century, surely it is time we stop allowing age-old prejudice to limit the level of care we offer our patients. This is not about Magical healing versus Muggle medicine. This is not about blood purity and principles. This is about providing the best care for every single one of our patients.’ She paused, her throat tight with emotion. ‘As healers, we owe the families of magical Britain the very best obstetric care we possibly have to offer. If I am given the funding and resources to develop this treatment, I firmly believe it has the potential to change or even save lives.’ She flicked her wand, ending the charm that projected her presentation and taking a step back from the stage. ‘Thank you for your time and your consideration.’

The silence in the Auditorium was deafening. Hermione couldn’t look up, couldn’t face the members of the board as she turned and began walking towards the door. She had almost reached it when, from somewhere near the back of the hall, there was a sound. It took her a moment to realise it was the sound of a single person clapping. A second later, another joined them, and another, and another. Gradually, the audience joined in, the unexpected swell of applause bringing fast, surprised tears to her eyes.

She looked up and found Harry and Theo standing together, cheering wildly, whistling through their fingers. She waved, relief coursing through her along with a swell of love so powerful she thought it might burst from her chest. But when she turned to find Malfoy, his seat was empty. Hermione’s elation faded, the smile dropping from her face.

He and Aurora had already left.



* * *



Fifteen minutes later, she stepped out of the hospital doors and into the sunlight, feeling like she’d been inside the building for days rather than hours. She closed her eyes, fighting to keep the emotion of the morning from bubbling up as she prepared to Apparate home alone. Theo and Harry had portkeyed back to Milan the moment her presentation was over, Harry having barely managed to convince Kingsley that his business in the UK warranted an hour's urgent leave. He’d promised her they’d be back within the next few days, but she’d fought not to cry as she waved them off, missing them before they’d even left.

‘Granger?’

Her stomach dropped at the sound of her name, her eyes flying open.

Malfoy stood at the bottom of the steps, unfairly handsome in a white shirt and chinos, a bouquet of pale pink roses in his hand. At his side, Aurora was fast asleep in her pushchair, the mangled head of one unfortunate flower clutched in her tiny fist.

‘Sorry,’ he muttered, holding the bouquet towards her. ‘I left these in the car. They look a bit shit now, all wilted from the heat.’

Hermione stared dumbly at him, unable to quite process that he’d come back. She’d been so certain that he’d left, upset by her choice of topic for the presentation. But Malfoy didn’t look upset. He looked…emotional, maybe a little overwhelmed. He also looked like everything Hermione needed in that moment, everything she wanted, if she was being completely honest with herself.

As though pulled by an invisible force, she began moving down the steps towards him, her heels clattering on the stone. She heard him drag his own breath into her hair, rough and jagged, felt his heart pounding in tandem with her own as they stood at the bottom of the steps and held each other, oblivious to the crowds of people moving around the Protego that arced overhead, keeping those Draco Malfoy cared about close, and everyone else out.

‘You were phenomenal in there, Granger.’ He murmured into her hair. ‘You’re fucking unbelievable, do you know that?’

Hermione couldn’t reply. She tightened her arms around his neck, breathing in his scent, just needing to soak in his proximity for a moment longer before she pulled away.

‘I can’t believe you came,’ she said when she eventually managed to release him.’ I - I was worried… when I didn’t see you at the end. I thought you’d left.’

Malfoy was silent for several seconds, frowning down at the ground as he clearly battled with himself. Hermione braced, waiting for the question she knew was inevitably going to come.

‘Your treatment?’ The words were hesitant, as though he wasn’t sure he even wanted to know the answer. ‘Blood transfusion… it couldn’t… it wouldn’t have…?’

‘No, Draco.’ She replied, as decisively but as gently as she could. ‘I truly don’t think anything could have prevented what happened that day. The curse was too advanced, too powerful.’ She swallowed past the lump closing up her throat. ‘I like to think it could have bought you some time, though. Enough to be together as a family, enough to say goodbye.’

He closed his eyes against her words. ‘If… if you can give that to another family, Granger, it would help them so much. You have no idea.’

‘That’s all I ever want to do. I just want to help.’

This time when he opened his eyes, they were wet, sparkling with unshed tears. ‘The world doesn’t bloody deserve you,’ he muttered. ‘I don't deserve you. I don’t deserve a person like you in my life.’

She watched the silvery droplets bead on his lower lashes, the urge to kiss them away almost as powerful as the urge to prove him wrong, to show him just how badly she wanted to be in his life.

‘Stop saying that.’

Hermione reached for him, taking hold of his wrist and lifting it so that they both looked down at the scarred lines of the word now imprinted beneath the pale skin of his forearm. ‘It isn’t true.’

She began gently tracing a finger over the raised white lines of each letter. ‘You’re a good man, Draco. I know you are. Whatever it is you want, you deserve to have it.’

Before she could even think about what she was doing, Hermione dipped her head, pressing a small kiss to the soft skin of his inner wrist. Malfoy froze, his entire body going still.

‘Tell me,’ she whispered, placing another kiss above the first, just over the letter M. ‘What do you want, Draco?’

He made a small sound, the fingers of his other hand coming up to gently wrap around her chin, tilting her face up to his. She lifted her eyes to find his pupils blown wide, the silver darkened to a gunmetal grey.

His lips parted, his tongue darting out to wet them.

‘I want...’

Hermione waited, her pulse thrumming in her throat, every nerve in her body vibrating with anticipation.

‘Fuck, Granger. I want…’

He didn’t move any closer, didn’t finish his sentence. He just… looked at her - staring at her mouth. Slowly, his thumb slid up to her bottom lip, and she shuddered as he ran the soft pad gently over the swell of it, pulling it down infinitesimally. She heard him swallow, felt him take in a deep, shaking breath against her chest. But he still didn’t move, and Hermione was going to burst into flames.

‘Please.’

She wasn’t even aware she’d said it until the word had tumbled out of her mouth. Malfoy twitched, the sound of her voice seeming to jolt him from whatever spell he’d been under.

‘Fuck,’ he muttered again, stepping back and blinking around dazedly, as though he’d only just realised they were still in public. He turned to Aurora, who was still fast asleep, her chest rising and falling peacefully, lashes splayed over her cheeks.

‘Fuck.’

When he looked back at her, the heat was gone from his eyes, replaced with something Hermione was much more familiar with - guilt.

‘I’m so sorry,’ he said. ‘I shouldn’t have -‘

Hermione’s heart was pounding, her cheeks flushed hot with a mixture of desire and mounting embarrassment. ‘No,’ she muttered. ‘ It was me. I - I shouldn’t have… that was… it was my fault.’

Silence fell, both of them looking down at the ground, at Aurora, anywhere but actually at each other.

Eventually, for the first time she could remember since they’d become friends, Malfoy was the one to break the silence.

‘O- okay.’ He stuttered, clearing his throat, ‘Now that your presentation is over… if you don’t have any plans.. can I uhh.. take you out for a celebratory dinner?’



* * *



In the end, Pansy and Blaise joined them for dinner.

Blaise had called Draco when they were on their way back to the car, and when he had told them where they were going, Pansy had immediately snatched the phone and informed him they were coming along. Malfoy hadn’t been able to say no, and secretly, Hermione thought they were both probably a little relieved. The day so far had been eventful, a whirlwind of emotion that had left them vulnerable, nerves exposed. It probably wasn’t the best time to be alone together.

Malfoy had booked them into a restaurant in Mayfair, a little French bistro with outdoor seating and an expansive wine list. The maitre d seemed to know him well and had no problem extending the table for Blaise and Pansy to join.

The afternoon passed quickly, the four adults enjoying the warmth of the late May sun, while Aurora determinedly thwarted all of Malfoy’s attempts to get her to wear a sun hat, ripping it off so many times that eventually he was forced to gently charm it to stay stuck to her head.

‘Why don’t you do that with her socks?’ Pansy asked, spearing a piece of lettuce out of her salad Nicoise. Malfoy appeared baffled for a moment, watching Aurora’s bare feet as they swung happily from the high chair, her socks already pulled off and discarded before the starters had even arrived.

‘That’s a bloody good question, Pans.’ He muttered, bending to pick one up off the restaurant patio. ‘I actually have no idea.’

They stayed at the Bistro for hours, Pansy and Hermione getting progressively more tipsy as Blaise repeatedly topped up their glasses of wine, the champagne Malfoy ordered only adding to Hermione’s alcohol infused haze. By the time they’d toasted their second glass in honour of her successful morning, she found herself firmly in the region of hammered, giggling whilst she tried not to miss her mouth with a spoonful of Crème brûlée.

Malfoy wasn’t drinking, watching them with an amused, only partially jealous expression as he nursed a soda and lime. Aurora had long finished her dinner of blended parsnip and carrot and had fallen asleep in the crook of his arm, her face turned into his chest.

Whilst he was distracted by a conversation with Blaise, and with the added inhibition of alcohol running through her veins, Hermione gave in and let herself really look at him across the table, her eyes roving over his features in a way she wouldn’t allow herself when she was sober. She tracked the sharp curve of his jaw, the subtle brackets on either side of his mouth when he laughed. She watched the muscle flex in his forearm as he rocked his baby, the prominent veins in the back of his hands when his fingers stroked over her soft blonde hair.

‘Take a picture, Granger. It’ll last longer.’

Hermione turned to find Pansy smirking wryly next to her, her green eyes dancing, only slightly unfocused.

She tossed her hair over her shoulder, affecting indignation. ‘I’ve no idea what you mean.’

‘Ha! You’re practically eye-fucking him across the table.’

‘I absolutely am not.’

Pansy snorted. ‘It’s okay, darling. I understand. I mean, he’s a beautiful man.’ Really, Hermione couldn’t argue with that, hadn’t it been exactly what she was thinking before Pansy had interrupted her staring session? She made a non-committal sound, taking a sip of her wine.

‘Anyone would think you might be a little bit in love with him.’

Hermione turned to Pansy, her eyes wide. ‘Shh! He might hear you. And anyway…I’m not… in love with Draco.’ Even to her own ears, the words sounded hollow. Was it possible to be in love with someone you’d never even kissed, she wondered?

Across the table, Malfoy’s eyes found hers, and he smiled, the crinkles appearing right on cue.

Yes. Hermione thought. It was definitely… possible.

‘I told you, Pansy,’ she insisted again. ‘We’re just friends.’



* * *



‘Right, come on then. I’ve paid the bill.’

Half an hour later, they were getting ready to leave. Malfoy had managed to wrangle Aurora into her pushchair without waking her, placing a white blanket carefully over her legs.

Watching him tuck Aurora in, Hermione slowly grew aware of her own mounting exhaustion. She’d had no more than four hours’ sleep since Harry had left, and now, with the beginnings of a hangover throbbing in the base of her skull, her eyes felt gritty, her limbs like lead.

Malfoy had clearly noticed, and he placed a warm hand on her arm. ‘Come on, love, I’ll take you home if you’re tired?’

She nodded, though the thought of her lonely flat made her heart sink.

‘Or we could do a movie night at the Penthouse?’ He suggested, shrugging off his jacket and placing it around her shoulders. ‘Theo’s got an amazing collection of Muggle DVDs and it’s still early. You don’t need to go home yet, do you, Granger?’

Hermione didn't think anything had ever sounded more appealing, and so ten minutes later the four of them made their way into the Penthouse. Malfoy closed the door, laying the keys to the Mercedes on the marble worktop before heading down the hallway to settle Aurora into bed.

She slipped off her shoes, moaning with satisfaction as she wiggled her toes, grateful for the reprieve after a day in heels. He watched her put it on, his gaze darkening as she pulled it over her dress, taking out her claw clip and shaking her hair free.

‘Okay?’ He turned to look at her, his expression uncertain. Hermione nodded, although her pulse was already quickening. She forced herself to relax, difficult when she could feel the tension rolling off him, too. She fixed her attention back to the movie, staring unseeing at the screen.

Towards what must surely have been nearly the end of the film, Malfoy’s wand suddenly vibrated on the table. ‘Draco,’ Pansy murmured, as Aurora’s first restless grumbles crackled over the monitoring charm. ‘The baby.’

Malfoy didn’t move. Hermione frowned, turning to look at him. He was usually up the second Aurora made a sound.

In the pale blue glow from the television screen, she could see that his head was tipped back, his eyes screwed tightly shut.

‘Draco?’ She whispered. ‘Do you need to go to her?’

Malfoy’s throat bobbed. ‘I can’t.’

‘What do you mean?’

He turned, eyes dark, jaw clenched. ‘I can’t, Granger. I can’t get up.’

Confused, Hermione opened her mouth to ask why, but then she felt Malfoy shift uncomfortably beneath her, and realised with startling clarity exactly how affected he was by the way they’d been sitting.

She inhaled sharply, shifting her legs in his lap. Malfoy bowed forward, a small, agonised whimper escaping through his teeth as he clamped down on her thigh. ‘Granger. You need to stay still.’

‘Everything alright over there?’ Blaise asked, and Pansy’s eyes narrowed, watching them.

Aurora’s cries grew louder, and Malfoy glanced up anxiously at the monitor, breathing hard. ‘It’s okay,’ she muttered. ‘I’ll go.’ As carefully as she could, Hermione slid her legs from on top of his and crawled out, careful not to pull the blanket off him. She tried her best not to look, but as she stood up, Hermione’s eyes instinctively dropped, helplessly drawn to the large, obvious projection tenting the material over his lap.

She stumbled down the hallway on wobbly legs, waiting for the heat that pumped through her bloodstream to recede as her heartbeat struggled to return to normal.

When she pushed open the bedroom door a minute later, Aurora was sitting up in her cot, wide awake. Hermione gently scooped her up, cradling her in her arms. The baby peered up at her in the dark, silvery eyes wide and glassy.

‘Shh, darling. It’s okay.’ She whispered, tucking Aurora’s arms into the blanket and rocking her gently. She bent her head and pressed a kiss to the soft swell of the baby’s cheek, breathing in the intoxicating scents of washing powder, warm milk and Malfoy. Hermione closed her eyes, suddenly feeling like she might be about to cry.

‘Oh, Aurora,’ she breathed. ‘What am I going to do?’ She took in a shaking breath, stroking the baby’s soft hair back from her face.

‘I think I might have fallen in love with your daddy.’

It didn’t take Hermione long to settle Aurora.

After rocking her for a minute or two, the baby turned onto her side and snuggled into the soft cotton of her father’s hoodie, one hand fisted under her chin, the other gripping tightly onto a sizeable chunk of Hermione’s hair. Hermione watched Aurora’s breaths turn steadily more even, her pink lips relaxing into a pout as she dropped back into a deep, contented sleep.

With one final tender kiss to her cheek, she lowered the baby back into the cot, extracting her hair from the clutch of Aurora’s twitching fingers like she was Bruce Willis defusing a bomb. She watched her breathe for a moment and then, satisfied she hadn’t roused during the transfer, Hermione left, closing the door partway on her way out.

The first thing she noticed when she re-entered the living space was that the movie had finished, the credits rolling silently along a black screen. The room was dark aside from the faint blue glow of the pool lights outside. Pansy was alone, pacing around the kitchen area. Malfoy and Blaise were nowhere to be seen.

‘Pansy?’

Pansy jumped, pressing a hand to her chest. ‘Oh! You’re back.’

Hermione stepped closer. Pansy’s face was ghostly pale in the shimmering light, her eyes wide and bright. ‘Aurora’s settled already? That was quick.’

‘Mmm,’ Hermione looked around, her senses immediately on high alert. ‘She’s fine… where’s Draco?’

Pansy glanced towards the glass doors that led to the balcony, where Hermione was just able to make out the shadowed outlines of Malfoy and Blaise through the window. Frowning, she stepped forward to get a better look.

Malfoy was leaning over the balcony, his head buried in his hands. Hermione could see immediately that something was wrong. His ribs were rising and falling with frantic, panting breaths, his hair wild, like he’d been dragging his fingers through it. Blaise was at his side, rubbing a soothing hand over his back, murmuring close into his ear.

‘Pansy,’ she whispered again, dread dropping into her stomach. ‘What’s happened? It’s not - oh god, it’s not Narcissa, is it?’

‘Oh,’ Pansy replied, pulling her gaze from the window. ‘No… It’s not Narcissa. She’s fine as far as I know.’
