Blaise looked on helplessly, still talking and talking, though Malfoy appeared past being able to listen.

‘I need to go to him.’

‘No!’ Pansy’s voice was loud in the quiet of the penthouse. ‘Draco’s okay. He just needs… just give him a minute, Granger.’

‘Pansy,’ Hermione warned, feeling like she might be about to cry. ‘You’re scaring me. What’s going on? What could have gone so badly wrong since I left the room ten minutes ago?’

‘Not wrong. Nothing's wrong. The opposite, actually. Honestly, it’s fine.’

Hermione’s temper flared along with her worry. She took a step towards the door, her frustration only increasing when Pansy moved to block her path, her hands held up placatingly. She pointed a finger at Pansy's chest, fear making her irrational. ‘So help me, Pansy, if you don’t tell me what’s happening, I’m going out there whether you like it or-‘

On the table between them, Malfoy’s wand suddenly vibrated, crackling to life as Aurora whimpered softly in her sleep. Two sets of eyes snapped to the wand, Hermione’s widening a moment later as understanding hit her with devastating force.

‘OH…MY…GOD.’

Pansy winced, her silence all the confirmation Hermione needed.

‘Oh god…He heard... He heard what I said, didn’t he? Wait… did you all hear what I said?’

Pansy nodded, her expression sympathetic. ‘Loud and clear… sorry, babe.’

‘Jesus fucking Christ!’

Hermione’s uncharacteristic language earned her two perfectly plucked raised eyebrows.

‘It’s alright,’ Pansy murmured, stepping towards her tentatively, like Hermione was a skittish animal, likely to bite. ‘Really, I think maybe it was for the best.’ When Hermione cut her a sharp look, she shrugged. ‘I think Draco really needed to hear it.’ She shrugged. ‘It’s obvious you two are sickeningly obsessed with one another. But truly, he was never going to be the one to acknowledge it, was he? The poor man is perpetually verbally constipated. It’s not his fault, of course. He’s never been allowed to ask for anything he wants. But now this has happened, there’s no pretending any more. Now he knows how you feel.’

Hermione looked outside to where Malfoy was now sitting on the ground, his back to the balcony wall, knees drawn up. He seemed to be finally talking to Blaise, but he was still gripping the front of his hair tightly, his expression conflicted.

‘Look at him,’ she murmured. ‘He’s horrified, Pansy. I won’t be surprised if he never wants to see me again.’

‘That man is not horrified.’ Pansy retorted softly. ‘He’s just a man who suddenly discovered that the woman of his dreams is in love with him, and is incredibly overwhelmed.’ Hermione exhaled slowly, dragging her eyes away from Malfoy to fix them on Pansy.

‘You should probably know that his thing for you isn’t exactly new, Granger.’ Pansy moved to sit on the sofa, patting the space next to her for Hermione to sit down. ’Before he was betrothed to Astoria, he was always going on about you, always staring at you. We all saw it, but I can safely say that none of us, especially him, ever imagined you’d reciprocate.’

Hermione couldn’t respond, her mind whirling as she thought back to Narcissa’s identical words that day at the care home.

‘Unfortunately, Draco is also fully aware of the exact chain of events that led you to him, so you can only imagine how much his heart is breaking right now, the guilt he’s feeling.’

Hermione couldn’t help the sob that rose in her throat. ‘I didn’t mean for this to happen, Pansy,’ she whispered. ‘I didn’t ever expect to find myself here. I…I just wanted to help… ‘

‘It’s not your fault.’ Pansy interrupted. ‘And it’s not Draco’s either. The fact is, if Astoria hadn’t died, your paths probably would never have crossed again. Draco would have remained married. He would have continued being the best father and husband he could have been under the circumstances, even if it wasn’t the life he chose. You need to know that about him, Granger. Draco Malfoy is the most loyal, unbelievably protective man you’ll ever meet. He’d die for the people he loves. He’d kill for them.’

Hermione thought about what she’d seen of Malfoy - the way he treated Aurora, his friends. She knew what Pansy was saying was true.

‘He loved Astoria.’ She murmured. ‘He did everything he could for her.’

Pansy nodded, shiny black hair swishing. ‘He did. The bond they had was special, definitely. But it wasn’t romantic love, Granger. For either of them. Don’t forget I was Astoria’s friend too, so I know. Certain things - husband and wife things, they were mandated - part of the contract. Draco has never experienced the feelings he has for you now. It’s all completely new to him, and that’s why he’s so… you know.’ Pansy waved a hand and Hermione nodded. She knew.

‘I just don’t want to seem like I’m trying to replace her.’ She admitted, hating herself for the selfishness beneath what she was saying, the fear. ‘Draco and Astoria had a whole life together. She’s Aurora’s mother. He still… he’s still wearing his wedding ring.’ Hermione forced the words out of her mouth, ashamed to admit that the ring meant so much to her.

Pansy nodded. ‘I think it’s a symbol for him - respect, memory, honouring the sacrifices Astoria made for him and Aurora. He’s tortured with survivor's guilt, fighting himself all the time. He doesn’t feel like he deserves the things she’ll never have.’

Hermione said nothing, her chest squeezing as she finally understood what the ring meant to Malfoy, why he felt he couldn’t take it off. Her heart broke for him all over again.



Outside, they watched Blaise lower himself to his knees on the balcony, pulling Malfoy into a tight hug. He rested his head on his friend’s shoulder, accepting the comfort, finally.

Pansy put a hand gently on Hermione’s arm.

‘I think you’re exactly what Draco wants and needs,’ Pansy smiled a little sadly. ‘You bring out a side of him I haven’t seen in so long. He can be vulnerable around you, he lets you see the soft, gorgeous heart he hides underneath all that shiny armour. And the way he trusts you with Aurora - It’s everything.’

Hermione inhaled a shaky breath. ‘That trust means so much to me, Pansy. I’d never do anything to hurt either of them. Draco and Aurora, to me they’re-‘

Pansy put a hand on Hermione’s arm.

‘I know. And Draco does too. That’s why he’s so-‘

Suddenly, Pansy froze, her eyes widening. ‘Ah shit, they’re coming back in. Quick! Act like you just walked back into the room.’

Hermione felt like she had whiplash. ‘Wh-what?’

Pansy was frantically wiping mascara from underneath her eyes, smoothing down her hair. ‘He doesn’t know that you know that he knows,’ she hissed, as though Hermione was meant to understand. ‘He won’t want you to see him upset. And he’ll kill me for saying anything to you. Just…be normal.’ Hermione stared at Pansy open-mouthed, her emotionally steam-rolled mind struggling to catch up.

The two men got closer to the door, their low voices rumbling through the glass.

‘Oh, god.’ Hermione frantically swiped at her own face, barely managing to plaster on what she hoped was an unassuming smile before Blaise pushed down the handle and he and Malfoy stepped inside, bringing with them a cool waft of night air.

‘Alright?’ Blaise asked, affecting his own mild expression. ‘Aurora all settled, Granger?’

‘Y-yes. She’s fine. She’s fast asleep.’ Hermione glanced up at Malfoy, who was hovering behind Blaise, staring down at the ground. He was pale, his nose pink-tipped. Hermione desperately wanted to hug him.

‘Sorry. We were just ah - there was umm, a seagull - outside.’ Malfoy muttered, his voice cracking slightly.

‘A…seagull?’

‘Yeah,’ he shrugged, still not meeting her eye. ‘It was uh, it was drowning in the pool. I was just helping Blaise rescue it.’ He nudged Blaise in the ribs. ‘Wasn’t I, mate?’

Blaise’s brown eyes widened incredulously, he bit his lip, trying and failing to hide his amusement as next to him, Pansy coughed to cover up a strangled snort.

‘Yeah, yeah. It was sort of…’ Blaise flapped his arms, mimicking what she thought was supposed to be a drowning seabird.

‘Oh,’ Hermione breathed. ‘Goodness. I hope it’s okay now?’

Malfoy finally met her gaze, silvery eyes red-rimmed, shining and beautiful. ‘Yeah,’ he murmured. ‘It’s…it’s okay now. It - uh - it’s good. Really good.’

Hermione smiled, and after a moment, Malfoy grinned back. The smile lit up his whole face, bunching his cheeks and revealing the dimples on either side. He stepped towards her, crossing the room quickly so that in a few short paces he’d closed the distance, pulling her into his arms and dipping his head to press his face into her collarbone. Hermione hugged him as tightly as she could, feeling him tremble against her as she ran one hand over his back, threading the fingers of the other through the soft blonde hair at the nape of his neck.

She thought she’d like to stay like that indefinitely, enveloped in Draco Malfoy’s arms, where everything felt less scary, where they didn’t need words, didn't need to face the difficulty of their situation. Because now the truth was out. Malfoy now knew exactly how she felt about him (even if they were both pretending he didn’t), and he was standing here, holding onto her like she was the only thing keeping him afloat.

‘We’re leaving now,’ Blaise’s quiet voice came from somewhere behind her. Hermione had almost forgotten they were there. ‘Pansy and I will walk you back.’

Reluctantly, she pulled away and Malfoy did too, letting his arms drop from her waist as he stepped back.

He watched her slip on her heels and then walked them to the door, kissing Pansy on the cheek before she and Blaise headed towards the lift, telling Hermione they’d wait for her in the lobby.

Hermione paused at the threshold, looking up at Malfoy, wondering if he was going to say anything about what he’d heard now they were alone. He stayed silent, aggressively chewing the inside of his cheek, his eyes fixed on her face.

‘Goodnight then, Draco,’ she murmured eventually. ‘I’ll call you tomorrow, okay?’ She stepped out into the Penthouse hallway, heels sinking into the plush carpet.

Malfoy caught her hand before she could walk away, long fingers enveloping hers and tugging her gently back towards him. Hermione stopped, her breath hitching as she turned.

His grey eyes were stormy, his jaw working in that way he often had when he was struggling to articulate what he wanted to say. She waited, breath held.

When he finally straightened, his expression was tight, the darkness in his eyes churning ferociously. He closed them and let go of her hand, stepping back to grip onto the doorframe tightly. ‘Goodnight, Granger,’ he whispered, turning away. ‘Sleep well.’

Hermione walked away from the Penthouse, once again frustrated, confused and aching with desire.

There was an owl waiting for her when she arrived home, the same owl she’d seen before, perched on the kitchen sill, its harness bearing the Hospital insignia. With a gasp, Hermione ran to the window and yanked it open, unwrapping the parchment from the owl’s leg with trembling fingers. She scanned it quickly, eyes widening as she read.



Hermione squealed with excitement, jumping up and down in her empty flat. The owl watched her with wary, yellow eyes for a moment before ruffling his feathers indignantly and flying away, seeming to have made his own judgment on the woman he’d delivered his post to. Hermione clasped the parchment to her chest as hot, disbelieving tears spilt down her cheeks, unable to quite process the words she’d just read.

Her first instinct was always to call Harry whenever she had any news, good or bad, so she surprised herself when she pulled her phone from her bag and found her finger hovering over Malfoy’s number on the display. She stared at the screen, every cell in her body urging her to call him, if only to hear his voice.

She had to force herself to cancel the call. It was too late to tell anyone now, her news could wait until morning.

Instead, she plopped down on the couch, the parchment clenched between sweating fingers and read it and reread until she must have fallen asleep, huddled in Malfoy’s hoodie, the blanket she now kept on the end of the sofa pulled over her bare legs.

Theo and Harry had returned from Milan, tanned and exhausted, with a successful arrest to Harry’s name and matching tattoos healing beneath their clothes.

‘Jesus, Harry!’ Hermione had exclaimed the first morning he’d returned, sitting on the sofa in front of him as he’d hooked two fingers into the waistband of his work trousers, dipping the material low enough that she wasn’t quite sure where he was going to stop. Just below his hip, where the toned muscle of his lower belly hollowed into the jut of his pelvic bone, Harry had tattooed Theo’s name in small, cursive letters, looping diagonally, dipping down towards his groin.

‘It’s beautiful,’ Hermione breathed, leaning closer for a better look.

‘And Theo has the same?’

‘Well, yeah,’ Harry pulled a face. ‘But it’s my name, obviously. It would be weird to have his own name tattooed next to his cock.’

‘Wait… you don’t seem opposed to the idea… Harry, do you like the thought of marrying Theo?’

Harry blushed handsomely, his cheeks almost matching the red of his Auror uniform.

‘I mean… I’m going to be thirty in a few weeks. I’ve never even come close to finding anyone as perfect as he is… I just…’ Harry sighed, flopping next to her on the sofa. He lay his head on her lap, nestling in close, folding his large body up as small as he could get it. ‘I’m a bit...’

He shrugged, turning so his face was buried so deeply in the pillow that she could barely hear him when he mumbled, ’scared.’

‘I…’ Harry swallowed noisily. ‘I’m scared of Theo,’ his voice was quiet. ‘I’m scared of how much I love him… it’s always been hard to hold on to the people I love, and… I just love him so fucking much, Mi. I can hardly stand it.’

‘Oh, Harry,’ she wrapped her arms around his ribs and squeezed him as best she could in the position they were in.

Hermione smiled and hugged Harry tighter, her heart almost bursting with joy even as she’d realised what this potentially meant for her. ‘Well then,’ she whispered. ‘Maybe it’s time you started thinking about how and when you’re going to tell him that.’

She prepped for her first meeting with the board, avoiding Ernie’s loaded questions and bitter insinuations about how she’d managed to convince the hospital to give her the full funding amount when his had been completely rejected.

Hermione forced herself to remain professional at work, refusing to engage in gossip about the presentations. However, she’d heard from Danielle that Ernie’s rejection was apparently because his results for a proposed pain relief potion had been skewed by unreliable data, rather than anything to do with her own funding.

She’d had several meetings with Healer Jennings, working out a new shift plan, timetabling her hours on and off the ward. She knew the next few months were going to be busy, but she’d never been averse to hard work. She threw herself into her planning, bubbling with excitement about what the opportunity could bring.

Padma had welcomed them warmly, ushering Malfoy and Aurora into the living room where she held her classes.

Hermione’s heart had squeezed at the sight of him in his pressed shirt and trousers, long legs stretched out, Aurora propped in between them as the two of them sat within a circle of mothers and babies, silently observing their surroundings, twin expressions of uncertainty on their faces.

He did well with the massage part, elegant hands deft and nimble as he followed Padma’s instructions, gently rubbing lavender oil over Aurora's chubby legs and kicking feet. He laughed quietly when she squirmed, continually carrying on a hushed conversation with his baby in what Hermione now realised seemed to be almost entirely whispered French.

When Padma brought out the coloured scarves, he began to look doubtful, and singing nursery rhymes turned out to be a hard no. Hermione had offered to take over and he’d gratefully vacated the circle, leaning against a wall at the back of the room, arms folded, eyes never moving from where Hermione sat with Aurora in her lap, singing and enthusiastically performing the actions whilst Aurora fisted her hands into Hermione’s T-shirt and nestled into her chest, peeping cautiously around at the other babies with a fascinated if somewhat suspicious expression.

Predictably, the other mothers were equally as fascinated with Malfoy, all clambering to engage him in conversation after the class. He sipped his tea politely, giving vague, one-word answers, widening his eyes at Hermione accusingly when she came back from chatting to Padma in the kitchen. Hermione was aware that every person there knew exactly who they both were, sensing the room’s attention on them as they left, knowing that they’d be the topic of conversation the second the door closed, everyone speculating exactly what the relationship was between them.

Hermione wished she knew.

Malfoy seemed to still want to be around her every moment he could. He was always finding reasons to touch her, always looking at her. He sat next to her whenever they had dinner with Theo and Harry or met up as a group. He wore her hair ties around his wrist, always made sure he saved her the last Percy Pig in the pack….

But…

He’d still made no attempt to talk to her about what he’d heard, and nothing more had happened since the day of her presentation. Hermione hadn’t been able to sleep, waiting for her phone to ring late at night, frantically checking for missed texts every morning when she woke up.

She knew it was wrong to feel frustrated, that she had no right to have any expectations of Malfoy when he was clearly still so fragile, still adjusting to his new life as a single father and widower.

But, Pansy had been so certain he felt the same way she did, and so had Ginny. Between the picture messages and the things he’d said, Hermione knew she hadn’t been imagining what was happening between them… or what had been happening between them.

Because now, she could only come to one conclusion - he’d heard her confession and realised that it wasn’t what he wanted. She wasn’t what he wanted, not in the same way she wanted him at least.

Hermione was a good person. She always did the right thing. And so, she forced down the sharp sting of rejection and pasted a smile on her face.

It looked like they really were just friends, after all.

‘Probably, at some point.’ Pansy admitted. ‘Because that’s what Draco does. But if it happens, you need to know it’s only because of how he views himself and his own self-worth, not a reflection on you. He’s obsessed with you. I watched him spend the whole of breakfast forcing himself not to eat you rather than his eggs Benedict.’

Hermione smiled through the twist in her chest. ‘There’s so much we still need to talk about,’ she murmured. ‘I know that what happened last night doesn’t suddenly mean that all the complications have disappeared. Draco still has a lot of healing to do.’

‘He does,’ Pansy said. ‘But truly, I don't think I’ve ever seen him look so at peace. Whatever this is between you two - it’s real, and it’s worth working for.’

Hermione was still trying to formulate a response when she felt Draco’s hand at her back, the warmth of his body as he stepped in close and dipped his head to her ear. ‘Ready to go?’

She nodded, reaching out to squeeze Pansy’s hand. Pansy squeezed it back, giving Hermione and then Draco one last kiss on the cheek before taking Blaise’s proffered arm and strutting out of the cafe.



* * *



They hadn’t made the conscious decision to walk anywhere in particular, but after leaving Bianchi’s, Hermione and Draco made their way down the street, heading towards the large lake that Hermione often jogged around, meandering slowly in the afternoon sun. Aurora had tired herself out at the cafe and dozed now, turned onto her side with her face buried in the plush fur of her blue dragon. Draco reached in to move the dragon down, tucking it under her arm and away from her face. The baby stirred when he bent to kiss her cheek, but her eyes remained closed, her little mouth falling open as the movement of the pram lulled her deeper into sleep.

Eventually, they found a quiet spot, just off the main path, under the shade of a large willow. Draco pulled out a blanket from underneath the pram, charming it to make it bigger before laying it out on the grass. Hermione sat while he busied himself setting more charms, a series of shields, repellents and even a variation of Hermione’s old monitoring charms over Aurora’s pram, before eventually dropping down on the blanket next to her, stretching out his long legs and leaning back on his hands.

Hermione turned to look at him, suddenly aware that it was the first time they’d technically been alone since she’d gotten out of the shower that morning. She peeked sideways through the curtain of her hair, her arms looped around her knees.

‘Hi.’

He smiled, grey eyes moving slowly over her face.

‘Hi, yourself.’

Hermione wasn’t quite sure which of them reached for the other first, but somehow, a moment later, she was in Draco’s lap, her sundress fanned out, knees planted either side of his thighs. Her fingers automatically found their way back into the soft curls of blonde hair at the nape of his neck, and his went straight to the small of her back, pulling their heaving chests flush together.

‘Hey! Not the hair.’ Hermione made a lame attempt at shoving his chest, which only resulted in Draco making a pleased, growly sort of sound and rolling them over so that he was on top of her, pinning her to the blanket as she gasped into his shoulder.

‘I love your hair,’ he muttered, nipping lightly at her jaw. ‘I love… everything about you.’

Around them, people walked past, formless blurs beyond the peripheries of Draco’s privacy charms. They didn’t exist. Nothing existed that wasn’t inside the safe, protective bubble of his magic.

Hermione pulled his face back to hers. She kissed him and kissed him, both of them growing more frantic until Draco wrenched himself away with a pained sound, insisting that he needed to stop.

‘I can’t come in my trousers in a public park.’ He panted. ‘It’s indecent.’

Hermione laughed out loud. ‘Oh, but almost coming in your trousers is okay?’

‘Lamentably, it's going to have to be.‘ He dropped onto his back and curled a hand around the front of her dress, pulling her into his side. Hermione tucked herself up against his ribs, slowly letting her heartbeat return to normal.

They stayed that way for a while, both lost in thought. Even as Hermione’s body began to relax, her mind whirred with a million questions - about the manor, about their relationship - all things that felt too difficult to bring up so soon in their new, fragile dynamic. ‘How are you feeling about Monday?’ She asked instead. ‘Are you looking forward to getting back to work?’

She felt him sigh, his chest expanding against her cheek. ‘Yes… I think so. I feel like I need to use my brain again, use my magic for something other than cleaning baby sick off my shoulder... But I’m not sure how I’m going to work it around Aurora. I know people are happy to babysit, and it’s not that I don’t trust them… it’s just…’

‘I know,’ Hermione replied. ‘It’s another big step.’

‘I fucking hate being away from her.’

Hermione stroked absently over the buttons down the placket of his shirt, feeling Draco’s fingers do the same through her hair. ‘Harry says you’re the best cursebreaker the DMLE has ever had.’

He grunted softly. ‘I’m experienced.’

‘You’re just going to consult from the DMLE office when you go back, though, right? You won’t be out in the field?’

‘It's the only way I can do the job now that I have Aurora,’ he replied eventually. ‘I’m not worried about getting hurt as such, but curse-breaking inherently requires regular proximity to dark magic.’ He brought one hand up to his mouth, chewing on a ragged thumbnail. ‘That’s not something I want to be bringing home to my baby.’

Hermione breathed out a sigh of relief. The thought of having both Draco and Harry out on missions simultaneously made her stomach lurch.

‘Maybe you could do something else?’ She hedged. ‘If it’s not the right profession for you any longer?’

He shrugged. ‘I’m good at curse-breaking. And it’s my way of repairing some of the damage my family did during the war. Who better than me to understand how dark magic works?’

Hermione looked down at the smooth skin of her inner arm, thinking again about the complexity of the spell work he would have used to take her scar, the suffering he’d endured as a result. Even the job Draco had chosen was another form of self-flagellation.

She took a deep breath, debating whether to broach the subject of the manor when, from inside her pram, Aurora began to stir.

He was up and off the blanket in seconds, reaching into the pram and lifting the grumbling baby out, murmuring soothing words into her ear. Hermione watched him from her place on the blanket, his blonde head bowed, shirt pulling tight over his biceps as he wrapped his arms around his daughter and held her tightly against his chest. She had to force herself to take a breath, almost ill with how much she wanted him.

They spent the rest of the afternoon sitting in the dappled sunlight under the willow, watching as Aurora grew closer and closer to learning how to crawl. She was already able to army shuffle, a slow half drag, half roll that she used to move around the blanket, making her way between them both to reach for the toys they held out. ‘Oh, God. I’m not ready for this.’ Draco said when she managed to get herself up onto her knees, rocking back and forth as she put forward one hand, then the other, on the cusp of moving forward before her knees gave out and she slid to her belly. He picked Aurora up and swung her into his arms, his expression somewhere between abject pride and utter devastation as he covered his daughter’s face with kisses. Aurora giggled and squirmed in his lap, her hands clasped either side of his face as she gamely attempted to eat his nose.

Later, Hermione and Draco lay on their backs as Aurora scrambled over them, smelling of suncream, strawberry yoghurt and Malfoy, happy now that her father had taken off her tights and her chubby little legs were bare.

When the time came for Hermione to leave and get ready for work, she was so reluctant that she actually did consider giving in to him and just quitting her job. Even when she’d previously been exhausted, mentally drained after several long shifts in a row, she’d never not wanted to go to work. However, the lure of staying with Draco and Aurora, the desire to wrap her arms around his neck and suck that plush, pouty bottom lip of his into her mouth - it was the closest she’d ever gotten to begging off sick when she wasn’t.

He walked her home slowly, evidently as loath as she was for them to separate. Hermione kissed him at the main door of her flat for a long time, both of them dragging it out so much that Aurora began to get grumpy and Draco had to bring her back out of the pram so Hermione could give her another cuddle.

When the time had veered into officially ‘late for work’, she forced herself to hand Aurora back and Apparated to her flat to change, pleasantly surprised that she hadn’t accidentally splinched herself on the way.



* * *



As it was, Hermione arrived at the hospital with two minutes to spare. Danielle met her at the front desk, already looking harried. The ward was particularly busy, and Hermione got straight to work checking notes and directing staff to mothers in various stages of labour. Ernie, who had also been called in as an extra pair of hands, stormed around the ward, rolling his eyes when Hermione asked him to help her lift a patient who was still struggling with mobility after an emergency cesarean. She forced herself to ignore his sour face as she chatted with the mother, settling the exhausted woman into a wheelchair while she held her new baby, following Ernie down to the small room where they conducted the newborn tests.

Later, when things had calmed down slightly, she sat behind the desk with Katie and Danielle, taking small, rushed sips of coffee whilst she filled in new patient scrolls.

‘He’s still not gotten over losing out to you on the research project, then?’ Katie muttered, jerking her head in Ernie’s direction. He was talking to a father in the waiting room, having still not spoken a word to Hermione their entire shift.

Hermione lifted her eyes briefly. ‘Clearly,’ she muttered. ‘I’m not giving it the slightest bit of attention. We have a job to do, if he chooses to act unprofessionally, then that’s on him.’

‘You know what’s actually unprofessional?’ Ernie snapped, having clearly overheard as he approached the desk, his green robes billowing behind him. ‘The ridiculous amount of flowers that were delivered to your office earlier. Ostentatious and unnecessary, and the time it took me to direct the delivery men to your room could have been better spent with my patients.’

Hermione blinked up at him. ‘Flowers?’

‘Like I said, an embarrassing amount of roses, taking over your entire office. Whichever Quidditch player you’re dating this time, he’s marking his territory like a bloody dog.’

Hermione bit her lip, unable to meet the rest of her colleagues' eyes as she gathered up her parchment notes and stepped towards her office, pushing open the door.

‘Oh!’

Ernie hadn’t been exaggerating. There were at least thirty bouquets of pale pink roses waiting for her, boxes and boxes of them - on her desk, the floor, the scent of them filling the small space and replacing the usual antiseptic smell of the hospital. Tucked into the ribbon tied around one of them was a card in a thick cream envelope. Hermione stepped forward and plucked it out, just as Danielle and Katie stumbled into the doorway behind her.

‘Hermione!’ Katie shrieked. ‘Oh, my god! Are these all from Viktor?’

Danielle nudged her roughly. ‘Shh. She dumped him ages ago…Who are they from, Hermione?’

Behind them, she could hear Ernie grumbling about ‘Distractions when there’s work to do,’ but he hadn’t moved from the front desk, and Hermione knew there were at least three staff manning the ward, which was calm for now. She pulled the card from the envelope, angling her body away from Katie and Danielle so she could read it.



Hermione swallowed, every nerve ending in her body tingling, burning with the need to Apparate back to the penthouse and kiss him to death. She took a deep breath, glancing up to find the wide, eager faces of Katie and Danielle.

‘Who are they from?’ Katie breathed. ‘God, it’s so romantic. Whoever sent them must be so in love with you, Hermione.’

She closed the card with a snap, fighting the urge to press it to her chest, pushing it into the pocket of her robes instead. ‘Come on.’ She said primly, breezing past them both. ‘We’ve got work to do.’

Hermione ignored the groans of her friends, unable to stop herself smiling as she made her way back to ward rounds, her cheeks aching, her chest aching too.



* * *



To Hermione’s great consternation, she didn’t leave the hospital until quarter past one. It was common for a shift to run over, but she’d been caught up in a delivery and hadn’t wanted to rush off after the birth. Then there had been the paperwork to complete and a nervous new father to comfort. By the time she landed back outside Hebridean Heights, she was exhausted and half mad with wanting to get back to Draco.

‘Hi, Jason,’ she murmured as he tipped his hat, pushing open the glass doors for her to enter.

‘Lovely to see you, Miss Granger.’ He grinned. ‘Bit late for a visit, isn’t it?’

‘Oh,’ Hermione indicated her robes, avoiding the real meaning of his question. ‘I had a long shift at the hospital tonight.’

She made her way towards the lift, grateful that it seemed to be in an amenable mood. It followed her commands promptly, delivering her smoothly to the penthouse on the first try.

Hermione hadn’t even raised her hand to knock before the door swung inwards, but it wasn’t Draco standing on the other side - it was Theo. His eyes were bloodshot, his wavy hair rumpled. He was in a T-shirt and joggers, the waistband slung so low that she could see the tattoo of Harry’s name below his hipbone.

It took Hermione only the briefest glance to realise that Theo was upset. His expression was tight, no sign of the usual effervescent smile lighting up his features when he saw her.

‘Draco’s not here.’ He muttered, pulling her over the threshold towards him. ‘We’ve all been trying to ring you. Narcissa’s care home called and asked him to get there as quickly as he could. He and Aurora left in the Merc about ten minutes ago.’

Hermione felt a cold wash of terror rush over her. ‘Is Narcissa alright?’

‘Not really. She’s having one of her episodes. They couldn’t settle her and none of her medication was working. Draco is the only one who can get through to her when she’s like that.’

‘Oh, God.’ Hermione whispered. ‘Okay. What should I do? Should I go to the care home?’

‘We’ll both go.’ Theo replied. ‘He’ll need you after this. I told him I’d wait for you.’ He bent to pull on some trainers, then stood, holding out his arm towards her. Hermione took it, feeling Theo’s other hand reach for hers in front of their bodies as he laced their fingers together, seeming to know that she needed the extra support.

A moment later, Theo and Hermione Apparated away from the penthouse together.

Chapter 26: Stand, by me


Chapter Notes

Hello!!

Ugh so this is a chapter I’m VERY nervous about, although it’s integral to the plot!

Please note, it comes with extra trigger warnings - although nothing explicit is described, there is mention of an attack, with further violence implied.

Thank you to everyone who has stuck with me through this journey. I’ve actually taken the chapter count off as I realise I need more time to execute the plan I have! There is a lot more of this story to tell!

Thank you for reading!

Nicky



See the end of the chapter for more notes



Theo and Hermione landed on the gravel path outside the gates of The Grove with a muted crack, small stones skittering under their feet. They crunched together towards the intercom set onto one of the stone pillars, where Theo tapped his wand on the gold faceplate, waiting until a voice crackled over the intercom.

‘The Grove.’

He leaned in close to the speaker. ‘Theodore Nott and Hermione Granger. We’ve come to meet Draco Malfoy?’

There was a brief silence on the other end of the line before the disembodied voice returned.

‘Mr Malfoy hasn’t arrived yet, but you both have previous wand clearance, so please, feel free to come inside and wait.’

Hermione felt her anxiety ramp up as she and Theo watched the ornate gates swing open. Of course he hadn’t arrived yet. He’d only left fifteen minutes before she’d gotten to the penthouse and the car journey was at least forty. She hoped he was driving safely, given the lateness of the hour and the worry he must be feeling.

Theo held her arm tightly as they crossed the courtyard to the large main doors, which buzzed open in readiness for them. Their shoes echoed over the shining tiled floors as, for the second time that day, Hermione’s nostrils were assaulted by the sharp, astringent smell of a hospital.

‘Good evening...’ Hermione recognised the healer at reception from her last visit, squinting at the name embossed into her gold badge as she and Theo approached the desk ‘…Pennie.’

‘Good evening, Miss Granger, Mr Nott. I hope you’re both well?’ Despite her worry, Hermione felt a brief swell of embarrassment as she took in Pennie Simons’ immaculate hair and pressed green uniform, knowing her own robes were crumpled and her braid like a bird’s nest after a long shift.

‘Fine, thanks.’ Theo replied distractedly, craning his neck down the corridor. The rest of the hospital was silent, blanketed by the heavy cloak of sophisticated silencing charms. He nodded in the direction of Narcissa’s room. ‘How is she? Any luck calming her down?’

‘I’m sorry I can’t comment on the status of a patient with anyone other than family,’ Pennie replied crisply, although Hermione didn’t miss the way her glossy lips quivered, her polite smile faltering just a little. Whatever was happening with Narcissa, Pennie was more rattled than she was letting on.

With nothing to do but wait, Theo and Hermione settled into two of the squashy leather armchairs in the waiting room, waving away offers of hot drinks and magazines. Theo held one of Hermione’s hands tightly in his own, his knee bouncing, blue eyes constantly flicking towards the main doors.

‘Does this happen often?’ Hermione asked him. ‘The healers needing to call Draco in?’

‘Oh…yeah, unfortunately,’ Theo shrugged. ‘Malfoy’s used to this. He usually comes on his own, but he’s always a bit wobbly afterwards, so I try to be here if I can.’

‘Right.’ Hermione swallowed thickly, her stomach churning. ‘You’re a good friend, Theo.’

Theo squeezed her hand in acknowledgement but said nothing further. Hermione thought that it was one of the most disquieting things about waiting, the fact that Theo was so anxious when he knew more about what to expect than she did.

In the end, they heard Draco’s arrival before they saw him - the sound of Aurora’s tired, croaky cries audible through the intercom as he buzzed for entry.

Hermione and Theo both stood as he pushed his way in through the doors, looking pale and harried. He carried a red-cheeked, puffy-eyed Aurora wrapped in a pink blanket, the tassels trailing almost to the floor. He sagged when he saw them, the taut lines of his face immediately softening with relief.

‘Hi,’ Hermione stepped forwards, wrapping her arms around his waist as he pulled her towards him with his free arm, dropping his head into her shoulder. ‘Thank you for coming,’ his voice was rough, muffled against her robes. ‘I’m really sorry about all this.’ Hermione barely had a chance to respond before he’d straightened, turning to Pennie, who was waiting at the entrance to the corridor.

‘Any change?’

Pennie shook her head. ‘I’m sorry, Mr Malfoy, no.’

Draco’s lips thinned, but he merely cleared his throat and nodded. ‘Alright. Take me down.’

Theo stepped closer, reaching out for Aurora. ‘Let me have Aurora, mate. We’ll stay here and Hermione can go with you.’

It’s alright.’ Theo gathered up the blanket, shifting Aurora to his shoulder as he turned and moved subtly further away. He waved a hand. ‘Go and see Cissa. We’re good, I promise. Currently, the fragile woman was bound in a form of Incarcerous charm, clearly there to keep her from injuring herself more than she already had. ‘It won’t,’ Hermione assured him. ‘I’m an experienced healer, Draco. There’s very little I haven’t seen before.’

His mouth twisted ruefully as he gave her a small nod before stepping inside the room, all eyes turning to him with a resounding sigh of relief. ‘This is healer Hermione Granger,’ Draco informed them brusquely, slipping off his coat and folding it over the chair. ‘She’s here at my discretion and will be staying to observe.’

A man whom Hermione assumed must be Narcissa’s chief healer stepped forward. He glanced at Hermione, his expression curious although he said nothing about her presence, deferring to Draco with a polite tip of his head. ‘Your mother is ready to go straight away, Mr Malfoy,’ he ran a finger down the notes he held in his hands. ‘We’ve given her all her potions, the second calming draught just went in.’

Draco nodded again, undoing his cufflinks and rolling the sleeves of his shirt. ‘How deep is she?’

‘As deep as we’ve ever seen her. Nothing we’ve done has registered any response.’

Draco leaned over her, his face pale and anguished, but his voice deceivingly calm. ‘I’m here now, mum,’ he murmured quietly. ‘It’s okay.’

Draco leaned over further, his body blocking Hermione’s view. She could hear him muttering, but it was too quiet to make out the words. Suddenly, Narcissa gasped, her whole body going rigid. Hermione instinctively stepped closer, moving so that she could see Draco’s face. She froze, sucking in a sharp breath.

Both Draco and his mother were now abnormally pale, their fingers linked tightly together. Draco’s jaw was clenched, his eyes no longer stormy grey but an unnatural translucent silver, unblinking and far away.

Hermione moved fractionally closer, although she knew Draco couldn’t see her. Whatever he was doing for his mother, neither of them was actually there.

On the bed, Narcissa started to breathe heavily, her exhales coming out in short, shaky bursts. ‘D-Draco?’ Her voice came out small. ‘I’m here. Find me, darling… please.’

Draco was also beginning to pant, his body shaking concerningly. Hermione watched the muscles flex in his forearms, his other hand twitching on the bed. He let out a low, agonised moan, and although his eyes remained wide open, Hermione watched a tear roll slowly down his cheek. ‘Mum,’ he whispered. ‘Mum?’ Hermione looked at the healers, apprehension slowly creeping in.

‘It’s alright,’ Narcissa’s chief healer insisted, even as Draco’s keening grew louder. ‘This is part of the process. It’s the same pattern almost every time.’

Draco was gasping now, his whole body trembling violently. Narcissa’s eyes had rolled back in her head, her gnarled fingers gripping Draco’s so hard that her knuckles had gone bone white.

Suddenly, Draco jerked, his knees buckling. The healers immediately sprang into action, sending cushioning and stabilising charms to stop him from falling to the ground. ‘No,’ he rasped, his eyes also rolling back so that Hermione could only see a small crescent of colourless iris below the wet line of his lower lashes. ‘Mum…no.’

Around the room, equipment began rattling, Hermione could feel Draco’s magic building, a sort of pressure, radiating from the bed and pushing out into the room’s atmosphere, dark and heavy, stealing Hermione's breath. ‘It’s happening.’ One of the healers warned. ‘He’s found her. Get ready, just in case.’ Several of the healers drew their wands, positioning themselves closer to Draco. Hermione tensed, her fingers curling around her own wand in her pocket.

On the bed, Narcissa began to cry. ‘Draco,’ she whimpered. ‘Do it….help me. Helpmehelpmehelpme.’

Draco began making a sound, a low groan emanating from somewhere in the back of his throat. It gradually increased in volume, his body taut as his mouth opened and blood began to trickle slowly from his nose.

‘KILL THEM, DRACO!’ Narcissa shrieked. ‘Do it NOW.’

The pressure in the room built. Draco’s nose gushed blood as a jumble of words poured out of his mouth. Hermione couldn’t make out all of them, and she was too frightened to even try. Panicked by the rapid, ragged sound of his breathing, she stepped forward, terror overriding every ounce of rationality as she watched Draco swaying on his feet.

‘Stay back!’ Narcissa’s chief healer called. ‘Don’t touch him! It’s incredibly dangerous to disturb the Legillimency process, Miss Granger.’

Hermione whipped around, glaring, her heightened emotions immediately getting the better of her. ‘This isn’t just simple Legillimency!’ She shouted. ‘Can’t you see it’s hurting him? Why is no one doing anything to help?’

Suddenly, Draco’s back arched, his body bending, his head falling back. The room felt as though it was shrinking, all the air sucked out of it at once. In the centre, Draco and Narcissa both fell deathly silent, their eyes blank, everything suspended as neither of them moved or seemed to take a single breath for a terrifyingly long time. Just as Hermione was about to lose her mind and pull out her wand, Draco managed a deep, shuddering inhale and then suddenly all the air rushed back in around them with a deafening roar.

Everyone seemed to start moving again at once. Draco stumbled away from his mother’s bed, a low sob wracking through him as he sank into the chair one of the healers pushed underneath him. He panted up at the sky, waving away the potions shoved under his nose as he struggled to catch his breath. ‘I’m okay,’ the words were rasping. ‘I’m okay. See to my mother, please. I almost didn’t find her in time.’

Hermione shook, trying to force her heart rate to return to normal as the room bustled around them, everyone back to business, as though the terrible thing she’d just witnessed hadn’t even happened. After a moment or two, Draco’s eyes found hers, bloodshot and red-ringed but thankfully returned to their original shade of grey.

‘Granger,’ he croaked as she crossed the room to kneel next to his chair. ‘A-are you alright?’

A healer leaned in, cleaning the blood from Draco’s face and shirt with practised efficiency.

He looked up at her guiltily, taking in her terrified expression, the tears drying on her face.

‘I’m sorry. I shouldn’t have let you stay.’

‘No,’ Hermione said quickly. ‘I’m glad. I’m so glad you did.’

She was interrupted again by a different healer appearing with a vial of blood replenisher, insisting, much to Draco’s irritation, that he took it as a matter of urgency. He flung it back, chasing it up with a dose of Pepper up and another potion that Hermione instantly recognised as a form of potent calming draught.
